Using $\alpha \in [ 0 . 2 , 0 . 9 ] \colon$ (range) η $| \ l _ { U } = \alpha - a + \Delta = \alpha$ and all Bernoulli levels lie in $\left[ 0 . 1 , 0 . 9 5 \right] ( \alpha - a \ge \alpha / 2 \ge 0 . 1 , \alpha + b \le 0 . 9 5 ) ; ( g l o b$ al infeasibility) for any threshold of coverage $t \geq \beta > g$ the slice is fully accepted, so $\begin{array} { r } { \varphi _ { 2 } ( t ) = \varphi _ { 1 } ( t ) - \Delta \rho = \varphi _ { 1 } ( t ) - 2 \varphi _ { 0 } \leq \varphi _ { 0 } - 2 \varphi _ { 0 } < 0 \mathrm { : } } \end{array}$ every floor-clearing threshold is risk-infeasible in $\bar { W } _ { \mathrm { 2 } } ; ( K L )$ only the mass-g slice changes and $w \equiv 1$ , so the per-sample $\mathrm { K L } \asymp g \Delta ^ { 2 } \asymp \varphi _ { 0 } ^ { 2 } / \beta$ and the n-sample $\mathrm { K L } \asymp n \varphi _ { 0 } ^ { 2 } / \beta ; ( L e$ Cam) $W _ { 1 }$ is feasible at $\beta ( \varphi _ { 1 } ( \beta ) = \varphi _ { 0 } > 0 .$ , coverage $\geq \beta ) ;$ if a valid procedure certified it with probability $\geq 1 / 2$ , then since validity forces refusal in $W _ { 2 }$ with probability $\ge 1 - \delta \ge 7 / 8$ , Pinsker $\mathrm { ( T V } \leq \sqrt { \mathrm { K L } _ { n } / 2 } \leq 1 / 4$ for $\mathrm { K L } _ { n } \leq 1 / 8 )$ yields a contradiction once $n \leq c \beta / \varphi _ { 0 } ^ { 2 }$ for an absolute c (indeed $\begin{array} { r } { P _ { 1 } \mathrm { ( c e r t ) } \le P _ { 2 } \mathrm { ( c e r t ) } + \mathrm { T V } \le \frac { 1 } { 8 } + \frac { 1 } { 4 } = \frac { 3 } { 8 } < \frac { 1 } { 2 } } \end{array}$ , ruling out equality too). The pair is built per $\beta \colon$ its $a , b , g , \Delta$ scale with $\beta ,$ and its relaxed frontier is $\beta ^ { * } = \dot { g _ { } } + a \Breve { g _ { } } / b \overset { - } { = } 3 \beta / 2$ (a constant relaxed slack $s = \beta / 2 ,$ never ↓ 0). The conclusion is a per-floor lower bound: for each $\beta \in [ \beta _ { - } , \beta _ { + } ]$ this two-point bound is $\Omega ( \beta / \varphi _ { 0 } ^ { 2 } )$ with no $s ^ { - 2 }$ factor; we make no single-fixed-world or single-lattice-point claim across the cell. □

Resolution of the coarse-grid pilot exponent. Sweeping $\beta$ across the cells of a coarse lattice mixes linear relaxed segments (Proposition 3(i), Le Cam lower-bound scale $\gtrsim s ^ { - 2 } )$ with constant-budget plateau segments (Lemma $^ { 3 , }$ per-floor two-point lower-bound scale $\Omega ( \beta / \varphi _ { 0 } ^ { 2 } )$ with $\mathrm { n o } \ s ^ { - 2 }$ factor), so a log–log fit across such a sweep can return a fitted slope strictly between −2 and 0. We regard this as a plausible discreteness explanation for the intermediate exponent seen in an early coarse-lattice pilot (not a theorem about the regression coefficient, whose fitted value also depends on cell widths, sampling weights, and censoring), and the coarse-lattice required-n behavior driving it is the archived pilot record (Appendix G: zero-margin cells with required- $n = \infty )$ . The attribution was registered as a prediction before the refined run: on a continuum-threshold profile with regular margin the fitted exponent was predicted to approach $- 2 \mathrm { : }$ ; on a coarse lattice, to flatten within cells. The refined registered family then returned slope −2.002 with 95% $\mathbf { C I } \left[ - 2 . 1 4 2 , - 1 . 8 6 2 \right]$ inside the pre-registered band (§5.1), consistent with Proposition 3(i); we treat the intermediate-exponent attribution as a registered, plausible discreteness explanation rather than proven. Zero-margin cells (required- $\cdot n = \infty )$ are the extreme of the plateau phenomenon and are observed empirically (Appendix G).

## E The Non-Derivability Analysis behind Table 1

Table 1 compresses a line-by-line analysis of whether each nearest verified prior line yields, by direct adaptation, any of the paper’s five objects: the hard floor as afinite-sample certified object under covariate shift, β-indexed lower bounds, the two-resource $( n , m )$ map, the localized accepted-region functional, and the nuisance-priced implementable upper bound. This appendix expands each row.

SCoRE [Bai and Jin, 2026]. SCoRE controls general selection-conditioned risks through weighted e-values, including a covariate-shift treatment with an estimated-weight robustness analysis. Adding a floor test to SCoRE plausibly recovers a U1-style validity statement (this is the concession that opens $\ S \bar { 2 } )$ , but nothing in the framework produces a lower bound, a feasibility frontier, or rate theory: the robustness analysis prices estimated weights into the e-value, not into a certifiable-slack threshold, and no quantity indexed by $\beta$ appears. The two-resource split cannot be read off either, because no result separates the labeled and unlabeled sample roles.

LEC [Wang et al., 2025b] and BalanceRAG [Jia et al., 2026]. LEC owns the ratio linearization this paper inherits $( R _ { Q } \leq \alpha \iff \mathbb { E } [ S ( L - \alpha ) ] \leq 0 )$ ; BalanceRAG owns cascaded two-dimensional lattice calibration with multiplicity accounting. Both are exchangeable: no shift, hence no weights, no frontier in $Q ,$ , and no β-indexed statement. BalanceRAG’s α-infeasibility declaration (refusing when no lattice point passes) is an honest-refusal mechanism but not a coverage floor: it certifies nothing about $C _ { Q }$ , and no lower bound accompanies it.

Weighted and marginal conformal risk control under shift [Tibshirani et al., 2019, Zecchin et al., 2025]. This family owns the weight machinery: weighted exchangeability, weighted CRC generalization bounds, and the estimatedweight repair toolkit [Yang et al., 2024, Kato et al., 2023, Wang and Goel, 2026, Laghuvarapu et al., 2026]. The certified estimand is marginal risk or coverage (not the selection-conditioned ratio), and no floor constraint co-exists with the risk statement. The complexity quantities that appear are global ESS-type functionals of w; the localized accepted-region functional cannot be recovered because no accepted set is distinguished by the analysis. No lower bound appears in the cited works of this line (we make no claim about the broader literature).

High-probability risk control under shift [Almeida et al., 2025]. We separate this work from the marginal line above, because its estimand reaches further than that grouping suggests. Theorem 9 gives high-probability control under target covariate shift for a conditional risk $\mathbb { E } _ { Q } [ \breve { L } ( X , Y ; \lambda ) \mid ^ { - } ( X , Y ) \in A ( \lambda ) ]$ , and instantiates the result for the false discovery rate; taking $A ( \lambda )$ to be the accepted set makes that object exactly the selection-conditioned ratio $\mathbb { E } [ L S _ { \lambda } ] / \mathbb { E } [ S _ { \lambda } ]$ we certify. So the ratio estimand under shift is within its reach, and we mark that capability present in Table 1. What does not follow is the rest of the map: the risk statement stands alone, with no hard coverage floor certified jointly against it, hence no second resource and no β-indexed lower bound; complexity is not reported through a localized accepted-region functional; and the estimated-weight treatment proceeds under an assumed weight accuracy rather than a finite-sample weight-estimation-error guarantee, so the nuisance is not priced as an explicit increment matched from below. The non-derivability claim for this line is therefore about the floor-indexed map, not about the ratio estimand.

Non-monotone CRC [Aldirawi et al., 2026]. The nearest line in distribution-free lower-bound theory proves a matching minimax lower bound for conformal risk control under non-monotone losses, using a Fano argument over a finite tuning-parameter grid. It is single-axis (excess risk in n) and floor-free; the analysis is extended to distribution shift by importance weighting, but no coverage-floor parameter appears, so there is no frontier, no second resource, and no unlabeled axis. Remark 12 details why neither statement implies the other despite the shared minimax toolkit.

Reject-option classifiers [Franc et al., 2023]; selective QA under shift [Kamath et al., 2020]. The reject-option line poses the floor as a population object: the bounded-abstention model fixes a coverage floor and minimizes selective risk, the bounded-improvement model fixes risk and maximizes coverage, and the optimal strategy is characterized (a Bayes classifier with a randomized selection function). It supplies no finite-sample certificate, no covariate-shift weighting, no β-indexed lower bound, and no labeled/unlabeled resource split; it is the population precedent for the object, not for any of the five finite-sample contributions here. Selective QA under domain shift [Kamath et al., 2020] studies exactly the deployment regime of our real-workload audit (abstain under shift to keep accuracy high), but with a learned calibrator and no validity guarantee or floor certificate, so it motivates the audit rather than competing with its certificate.

Inherited machinery, credited. We inherit and do not claim: weighted e-values and learn-then-test multiplicity [Angelopoulos et al., 2021, Laufer-Goldshtein et al., 2023], cascade multiplicity accounting [Jia et al., 2026], the ratio linearization [Wang et al., 2025b], empirical-Bernstein and betting-based concentration [Maurer and Pontil, 2009, Waudby-Smith and Ramdas, 2024], and the Le Cam two-point technique [Tsybakov, 2009]. What is new is not the floor as a population object (the reject-option line [Franc et al., 2023] already poses guaranteed-coverage selective risk) but the floor as afinite-sample certified object under covariate shift, and everything the floor then creates: the frontier, the β-indexed two-resource map, the localized functional, and the budgeted-nuisance implementable upper bound.

This appendix proves Theorems 2 and 3 and Proposition 1, and records the separation of the budgeted certificate from the trivial worst-case-box baseline. The setting is Assumption 1 with the four-block splitting of Algorithm 1: the labeled source sample splits into $D _ { w } ^ { P } \left( \mathrm { s i z e } n _ { w } \right)$ and $D _ { r } \ ( \mathrm { s i z e } \ n _ { r } )$ ; the unlabeled target sample splits into $\bar { D } _ { w } ^ { Q }$ (size $m _ { w } )$ and $D _ { f }$ (size $m _ { f } ) ;$ all four blocks are mutually independent, and only covariates are used from $D _ { w } ^ { P }$ . Throughout, $A _ { \lambda }$ denotes the union of cells with $S _ { \lambda } = 1 , K _ { \lambda } = | A _ { \lambda } | \kappa$ the number of accepted cells, and $Z _ { i } ^ { \lambda } = \hat { w } ( X _ { i } ) S _ { \lambda } ( X _ { i } ) ( L _ { i } - \alpha )$ the risk-test variable on $D _ { r }$ , with range contained in $\lceil - \alpha B , ( 1 - \alpha ) B \rceil$ of width $\le B$ . Standing parameter domain: $\delta \le 1 / 8$ (the standing confidence of Theorem 1), $B \geq 2 , \alpha \in [ 0 , 1 ]$ (and $0 < \alpha < 1$ wherever a betting denominator appears, Proposition 5), $s , \kappa > 0 , 0 < c _ { 0 } \leq 1$ , and all empirical-Bernstein sample sizes $n _ { r } , m _ { f } , n _ { w } , m _ { w } \ge 2$ (the Maurer–Pontil endpoints carry $N - 1$ denominators). The power-side radii carry $\log ( 2 / \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } ) = \log ( 4 / \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } ) = \log ( 1 6 | \Lambda | / \delta )$ ; under $\delta \le 1 / 8$ (so $\log ( | \Lambda | / \delta ) \ge \log 8 )$ these are $\leq \ \frac { 7 } { 3 } \log ( | \Lambda | / \delta )$ , absorbed into the displayed absolute constant $C _ { i }$ , so conditions written with $\log ( | \Lambda | ^ { \top } \delta )$ are correct up to $C$ . The budgets are

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \rho _ { \lambda } = 2 \Big [ \sqrt { \frac { K _ { \lambda } \bar { q } ( A _ { \lambda } ) } { m _ { w } } } + \sqrt { \frac { 2 L _ { w } } { m _ { w } } } \Big ] + 2 B \Big [ \sqrt { \frac { K _ { \lambda } \bar { p } ( A _ { \lambda } ) } { n _ { w } } } + \sqrt { \frac { 2 L _ { w } } { n _ { w } } } \Big ] , \qquad L _ { w } : = \log \frac { 4 ( | \Lambda | + 1 ) } { \delta _ { w } } , } \end{array}\tag{6}
$$

with the plug-in correction $\tilde { q } ( A ) : = 2 \hat { q } ( A ) + 4 L _ { w } / m _ { w }$ and $\tilde { p } ( A ) : = 2 \hat { p } ( A ) + 4 L _ { w } / n _ { w } ;$ Proposition 1(iii) guarantees $q ( A ) \leq { \tilde { q } } ( A )$ and $p ( A ) \leq \tilde { p } ( A )$ on the same event, and the simultaneity log-factor $L _ { w }$ is carried in full. We write $r _ { \lambda } : = \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ | \hat { w } - w | S _ { \lambda } ]$ for the true localized weight error and keep $\rho _ { \lambda }$ (the right-hand side of (6)) for the computable radius; the good event is $E _ { w } = \{ \forall \lambda : ~ r _ { \lambda } \leq \bar { \rho _ { \lambda } } \}$ , and every computable risk test uses the margin $- \rho _ { \lambda }$ , never the unknown true error $r _ { \lambda }$

## F.1 Proof of Proposition 1 (stratified-shift histogram estimator)

Proposition (I1, restated). Under Assumption 1, with $\hat { p } , \hat { q }$ the empirical cellfrequenciesfrom $n _ { w }$ source and $m _ { w }$ target draws and $\hat { w } _ { k } = \mathrm { c l i p } ( \hat { q } _ { k } / \hat { p } _ { k } , 0 , B )$ (with $\hat { w } _ { k } : = B$ when $\hat { p } _ { k } = 0 ;$ , as in Algorithm 1, so wˆ is defined on every outcome):

with probability $\ge 1 - \delta _ { w }$ , simultaneously for every region $A \in \{ A _ { \lambda } \} _ { \lambda \in \Lambda } \cup \{ \mathcal { X } \}$

$$
\mathbb { E } _ { P } \big [ \big | { \hat { w } } - w \big | \mathbf { 1 } _ { A } \big ] = \sum _ { { k \colon } \mathcal { X } _ { k } \subseteq A } p _ { k } \big | { \hat { w } } _ { k } - w _ { k } \big | \le 2 \Big [ \sqrt \frac { | A | \kappa q ( A ) } { m _ { w } } + \sqrt \frac { 2 L _ { w } } { m _ { w } } \Big ] + 2 B \Big [ \sqrt \frac { | A | \kappa p ( A ) } { n _ { w } } + \sqrt \frac { 2 L _ { w } } { n _ { w } } \Big ] ,
$$

and (iii) the same bound holds with $( p , q )$ replaced by $( \tilde { p } , \tilde { q } )$ , making the budgets (6) computable.

Proof. Step 1 (good event). By multiplicative Chernoff, $\mathbb { P } ( \hat { p } _ { k } \ < \ p _ { k } / 2 ) \ \le \ \exp ( - n _ { w } p _ { k } / 8 ) \ \le \ \delta _ { w } / ( 4 K )$ under Assumption 1(ii); a union over k gives $\hat { p } _ { k } \ge p _ { k } / 2$ for all cells with probability $\ge 1 - \delta _ { w } / 4$

Step 2 (decomposition). On that event, for each $k ,$ since clipping to $[ 0 , B ] \ni w _ { k }$ can only help,

$$
p _ { k } | \hat { w } _ { k } - w _ { k } | \le p _ { k } \Big | \frac { \hat { q } _ { k } } { \hat { p } _ { k } } - \frac { q _ { k } } { p _ { k } } \Big | = \frac { p _ { k } } { \hat { p } _ { k } } \Big | ( \hat { q } _ { k } - q _ { k } ) - w _ { k } ( \hat { p } _ { k } - p _ { k } ) \Big | \le 2 | \hat { q } _ { k } - q _ { k } | + 2 B | \hat { p } _ { k } - p _ { k } | .
$$

Step 3 (simultaneous localized $\ell ^ { 1 }$ concentration). For a fixed union-of-cells region A (the bound below is then made simultaneous over the stated family), write $\begin{array} { r } { D _ { A } = \sum _ { k \in A } \left| \hat { q } _ { k } - q _ { k } \right| } \end{array}$ . By Jensen and Cauchy–Schwarz, $\mathbb { E } D _ { A } \leq$ $\begin{array} { r } { \sum _ { k \in { \cal A } } \sqrt { q _ { k } / m _ { w } } \le \sqrt { | { \cal A } | _ { \mathcal { K } } q ( A ) / m _ { w } } } \end{array}$ $D _ { A }$ has bounded differences $2 / m _ { w }$ in each target draw, so McDiarmid’s inequality gives $D _ { A } \ \leq \ \mathbb { E } D _ { A } + \sqrt { 2 \log ( 1 / \varepsilon ) / m _ { w } }$ with probability $\geq 1 - \varepsilon$ (we set $\varepsilon = \delta _ { w } / ( 4 ( | \Lambda | + 1 ) )$ per region below). To keep both localization and simultaneity rigorous, apply McDiarmid separately to the $| \Lambda | + 1$ regions $\{ A _ { \lambda } \} _ { \lambda \in \Lambda } \cup \{ \mathcal { X } \}$ at level $\delta _ { w } / ( 4 ( | \Lambda | + 1 ) )$ ) each: the deviation term becomes $\sqrt { 2 L _ { w } / m _ { w } }$ with $L _ { w } = \log ( 4 ( | \Lambda | + 1 ) / \delta _ { w } )$ which is exactly the form carried in (6). The same argument applies to the source frequencies with prefactor $2 B$ and denominator $n _ { w }$

Step 4 (combine). Sum the Step-2 bound over $k \in A$ and apply the Step-3 bounds to the two $\ell ^ { 1 }$ terms.

(iii) Plug-in correction. For $Y = m _ { w } \hat { q } ( A ) \sim \mathrm { B i n } ( m _ { w } , q ( A ) )$ , the event $\{ q ( A ) > 2 \hat { q } ( A ) + 4 L _ { w } / m _ { w } \}$ equals $\{ Y <$ $m _ { w } q ( A ) / 2 - 2 L _ { w } \}$ . If $m _ { w } q ( A ) < 4 L _ { w }$ this event is empty (its threshold $m _ { w } q ( A ) / 2 - 2 L _ { w } < 0$ while $\bar { Y } \geq \mathsf { \bar { 0 } } )$ , so assume $m _ { w } q ( A ) \geq 4 L _ { w } .$ whence u $: = m _ { w } q / 2 + 2 L _ { w } \leq m _ { w } q$ lies in the lower-tail domain. By the Bernstein–Chernoff lower tail $\mathbb { P } ( Y \le m _ { w } q - u ) \le \exp ( - u ^ { 2 } / ( 2 m _ { w } q ) )$ , and AM–GM giving $u ^ { 2 } \geq 4 \cdot ( m _ { w } q / 2 ) ( 2 L _ { w } ) = 4 m _ { w } q L _ { w } .$ , the probability is $\leq e ^ { - 2 L _ { w } } \leq ( \bar { \delta } _ { w } / ( 4 ( | \hat { \Lambda } | + \bar { 1 } ) ) ) ^ { 2 }$ , comfortably within the per-region allocation; likewise for $p ( A )$ with $n _ { w }$ (the event empty when $n _ { w } p ( A ) < 4 L _ { w } )$ . Call $G _ { w }$ the intersection of the full collection of good events (Chernoff good event, per-region McDiarmid for two marginals, per-region plug-in for two marginals); its complement stays within $\delta _ { w } , \mathrm { i . e . } \operatorname { \mathbb { P } } ( \bar { G } _ { w } ) \geq 1 - \delta _ { w } ,$ , and on $G _ { w }$ all of the Step-1, Step-3, and part-(iii) deviation bounds (in particular the two-sided cellwise controls $| \hat { q } ( A ) - q ( A ) | \leq D _ { A } ^ { q } , | \hat { p } ( \hat { A } ) - p ( \hat { A } ) | \leq D _ { A } ^ { \hat { p } }$ used by the power-side bridge below) hold simultaneously. Substituting $\tilde { q } ( A ) \geq q ( A )$ and ${ \tilde { \tilde { p } } } ( A ) \geq p ( A )$ into the Step-3/4 bound yields computable budgets dominating the population bound on the same event. □

Rate remark. Globally $( A = { \mathcal { X } } )$ the computable radius scales as $O \big ( \sqrt { ( K + L _ { w } ) / m _ { w } } + B \sqrt { ( K + L _ { w } ) / n _ { w } } \big )$ in the regime $m _ { w } , n _ { w } \gtrsim$ min $\{ K , L _ { w } \}$ (otherwise the plug-in cross terms $\sqrt { K L _ { w } } / m _ { w }$ and $B \sqrt { K L _ { w } } / n _ { w } ,$ , from $\tilde { q } , \tilde { p }$ at $A = \bar { \mathcal { X } }$ , add; an upper bound, with the $L _ { w }$ simultaneity term shown explicitly; we reserve ≍ for a matched two-sided rate), the $\sqrt { K / N }$ rate of a K-parameter shift model up to the confidence term; the localized budgets shrink with the accepted masses $q ( A _ { \lambda } ) , p ( A _ { \lambda } )$ : the nuisance, like the variance, is an accepted-region quantity. The true error $r _ { \lambda }$ can be smaller (even zero for matched histograms); only the radius $\rho _ { \lambda }$ is rate-controlled.

Lemma 4 (Empirical-Bernstein endpoint with the biased variance). Let $Z , Z _ { 1 } , \ldots , Z _ { n }$ be i.i.d. in $[ 0 , 1 ]$ with $n \geq 2 ,$ , let $\gamma \in ( 0 , 1 )$ , put $L : = \log ( 2 / \gamma )$ , and let $\begin{array} { r } { \hat { V } : = \frac { 1 } { n } \sum _ { i } ( Z _ { i } - \bar { Z } ) ^ { 2 } } \end{array}$ be the biased empirical variance. Then with probability at least $1 - \gamma ,$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } [ Z ] \le \bar { Z } + \sqrt { \frac { 2 \hat { V } L } { n } } + \frac { 7 L } { 3 ( n - 1 ) } . } \end{array}\tag{7}
$$

Proof. If $7 L / ( 3 ( n - 1 ) ) \geq 1$ the right-hand side is at least $\bar { Z } + 1 \ge \mathbb { E } [ Z ]$ and there is nothing to prove; assume $7 L / ( 3 ( n - \dot { 1 } ) ) < 1$ and set $x : = L / \bar { n } < L / ( n - 1 ) < 3 / 7$ . Split the confidence asymmetrically: $\delta _ { A } \ \dot { : } = e ^ { - L + x }$ and $\delta _ { B } : = e ^ { - L } ( \dot { 2 } - e ^ { x } )$ , both positive since $x < 3 / 7 < \log 2$ , with $\delta _ { A } + \delta _ { B } = 2 e ^ { - L } = \gamma$ . Write $a : = \log ( 1 / \delta _ { A } ) =$ $L ( n - 1 ) / n$ and $b : = \log ( \bar { 1 / \delta } _ { B } ) = L - \log ( 2 - e ^ { x } )$ . Bernstein’s inequality for variables of range 1 at level $\delta _ { A }$ gives $\mathbb { E } [ Z ] - \bar { Z } \le \sqrt { 2 \sigma ^ { 2 } a / n } + a / ( 3 n )$ with $\sigma ^ { 2 } = \operatorname { V a r } ( Z )$ ; the variance bound of Maurer and Pontil [2009] at level $\delta _ { B }$ gives $\sigma \leq { \sqrt { V } } + { \sqrt { 2 b / ( n - 1 ) } }$ with $\begin{array} { r } { V : = \frac { 1 } { n - 1 } \sum _ { i } ( Z _ { i } - \bar { Z } ) ^ { 2 } = \frac { n } { n - 1 } \hat { V } } \end{array}$ . A union bound costs $\delta _ { A } + \delta _ { B } = \gamma$ , and on the intersection

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } [ Z ] - \bar { Z } \ \le \ \sqrt { \frac { 2 V a } { n } } + 2 \sqrt { \frac { a b } { n ( n - 1 ) } } + \frac { a } { 3 n } , \qquad \mathrm { w h e r e } \qquad \frac { 2 V a } { n } \ = \ \frac { 2 } { n } \cdot \frac { n \hat { V } } { n - 1 } \cdot \frac { L ( n - 1 ) } { n } = \ \frac { 2 \hat { V } L } { n } , } \end{array}
$$

so the root term is exactly the one in (7): the asymmetric split is chosen precisely to make a cancel the $n / ( n - 1 )$ of the unbiased variance. It remains to bound the two additive terms by $7 L / ( 3 ( n - 1 ) )$ , i.e. to show $\begin{array} { r } { C : = 2 \frac { n - 1 } { n } \sqrt { b / L } + } \end{array}$ $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { ( n - 1 ) ^ { 2 } } { 3 n ^ { 2 } \atop . } \leq \frac { 7 } { 3 } } \end{array}$ . Since $- \log ( 2 - e ^ { x } ) \leq 2 x \mathrm { o n } [ 0 , 3 / 7 ]$ we have $b \leq L + 2 x = L ( 1 + 2 / n )$ , hence $\sqrt { b / L } \leq \sqrt { 1 + 2 / n } \leq$ $1 + 1 / n$ and

$$
\begin{array} { r } { C \ \leq \ 2 \frac { n - 1 } { n } \big ( 1 + \frac { 1 } { n } \big ) + \frac { ( n - 1 ) ^ { 2 } } { 3 n ^ { 2 } } \ = \ \frac { 7 n ^ { 2 } - 2 n - 5 } { 3 n ^ { 2 } } \ = \ \frac { 7 } { 3 } - \frac { 2 } { 3 n } - \frac { 5 } { 3 n ^ { 2 } } \ < \ \frac { 7 } { 3 } . } \end{array}
$$

Remark 14 (Released kernel: the registered per-cell radius). The radius analyzed above, (6), unions over the $| \Lambda | + 1$ regions $\{ A _ { \lambda } \} _ { \lambda \in \Lambda } \cup \{ \mathcal { X } \}$ , two McDiarmid events per region (one per marginal), which is the source of its $L _ { w } =$ $\log ( 4 ( | \Lambda | + \bar { 1 } ) / \delta _ { w } )$ . The released implementation computes a different registered closed form, written $\rho _ { \lambda } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } }$ , which unions over cells instead. With per-event confidence $\gamma _ { w } = \delta _ { w } / ( 4 K )$ and $\bar { L _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v } 2 } } : = \log ( 2 / \gamma _ { w } ) = \log ( 8 K / \delta _ { w } )$ , set the empirical-Bernstein mass bands

$$
\begin{array} { r l r l } & { e _ { k } ^ { P } = \sqrt { \frac { 2 \hat { p } _ { k } \left( 1 - \hat { p } _ { k } \right) L _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v } 2 } } { n _ { w } } } + \frac { 7 L _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v } 2 } } { 3 ( n _ { w } - 1 ) } , } & & { e _ { k } ^ { Q } = \sqrt { \frac { 2 \hat { q } _ { k } \left( 1 - \hat { q } _ { k } \right) L _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v } 2 } } { m _ { w } } } + \frac { 7 L _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v } 2 } } { 3 ( m _ { w } - 1 ) } , } \end{array}
$$

put $\tilde { p } _ { k } : = \operatorname* { m a x } \{ \hat { p } _ { k } - e _ { k } ^ { P } , 0 \}$ and let the per-cell ratio error be er $\mathrm { r } _ { k } : = B$ when $\tilde { p } _ { k } = 0$ and $\mathrm { e r r } _ { k } : = \operatorname* { m i n } \{ B , \ ( e _ { k } ^ { Q } +$ $\hat { w } _ { k } e _ { k } ^ { P } ) / \tilde { p } _ { k } \}$ otherwise; then

$$
\rho _ { \lambda } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } } = \sum _ { k : \chi _ { k } \cap A _ { \lambda } \neq \emptyset } ( \hat { p } _ { k } + e _ { k } ^ { P } ) \mathrm { e r r } _ { k } .\tag{8}
$$

The good event. Let $G _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } }$ be the event that all 4K one-sided mass deviations obey their bands $( K$ cells × two marginals × two sides, at level $\gamma _ { w }$ each, so the union costs $4 K \gamma _ { w } = \delta _ { w } )$ . Cell indicators are [0, 1]-valued and the bands above are exactly the endpoint of Lemma 4 at $\gamma _ { w } .$ , evaluated at the biased empirical variance $\hat { p _ { k } } ( 1 - \hat { p } _ { k } )$ that the released code uses; that lemma is stated for this normalization, so per cell and by the union, $\mathbb { P } ( \bar { G } _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } } ) \geq \bar { 1 } - \delta _ { w }$ . The same lemma covers the released risk UCB and floor LCB, which use the same $( \dot { 1 } / n )$ convention.

Domination on $G _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } }$ . Fix a cell k and write $u _ { k } = \hat { q } _ { k } / \hat { p } _ { k }$ when $\hat { p } _ { k } > 0 . \mathrm { I f } \tilde { p } _ { k } = 0$ (in particular when $\hat { p } _ { k } = 0$ , where the algorithm sets $\hat { w } _ { k } = B )$ the branch $\mathrm { e r r } _ { k } = B$ applies and $| \hat { w } _ { k } - w _ { k } | \le B$ because $\boldsymbol { \bar { w _ { k } } } , \boldsymbol { \hat { w } _ { k } } \in [ 0 , B ]$ ]. Otherwise $\hat { p } _ { k } > 0$ and there are two cases. If $u _ { k } \le B$ then $\hat { w } _ { k } = u _ { k } .$ , and the identity ${ \hat { q } } _ { k } p _ { k } - q _ { k } { \hat { p } } _ { k } = { \hat { p } } _ { k } { \left( { \hat { q } } _ { k } - q _ { k } \right) } + { \hat { q } } _ { k } { \left( p _ { k } - { \hat { p } } _ { k } \right) }$ gives $\vert \hat { w } _ { k } - w _ { k } \vert \leq ( e _ { k } ^ { Q } + \hat { w } _ { k } e _ { k } ^ { P } ) / p _ { k } . \mathrm { ~ I f ~ } u _ { k } > B$ then $\hat { w } _ { k } = B \ge w _ { k }$ (Model B<sup>′</sup> assumes $w \leq B )$ and $B \hat { p } _ { k } - \hat { q } _ { k } < 0 .$ so $p _ { k } ( B - w _ { k } ) = B p _ { k } - q _ { k } \leq B | p _ { k } - \hat { p } _ { k } | + | \hat { q } _ { k } - q _ { k } | \leq B e _ { k } ^ { P } + e _ { k } ^ { Q }$ , giving the same bound. In both cases $p _ { k } \ge \tilde { p } _ { k }$ on $G _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } } , \mathrm { s o } \left| \hat { w } _ { k } - w _ { k } \right| \leq ( e _ { k } ^ { Q } + \hat { w } _ { k } e _ { k } ^ { P } ) / \tilde { p } _ { k }$ , and since $| \hat { w } _ { k } - w _ { k } | \le B$ always, $| \hat { w } _ { k } - w _ { k } | \le \mathrm { e r r } _ { k }$ for every cell. Because wˆ and w are constant on cells, and $P ( \mathcal X _ { k } \cap A _ { \lambda } ) \leq p _ { k } \leq \hat { p } _ { k } + e _ { k } ^ { P }$ on $G _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } }$

$$
r _ { \lambda } \ = \ \sum _ { k : \chi _ { k } \cap A _ { \lambda } \neq \emptyset } P ( \chi _ { k } \cap A _ { \lambda } ) \left| \hat { w } _ { k } - w _ { k } \right| \ \leq \ \rho _ { \lambda } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } } .
$$

This covers the union-of-cells acceptance regions Assumption 1(iii) requires and, as an extension beyond that assumption, lattices whose $A _ { \lambda }$ cuts cell interiors: each intersecting cell is charged its full upper mass, which over-counts and is therefore conservative.

What transfers, and what does not. Theorem 2 uses the radius only through the domination event $E _ { w } = \{ \forall \lambda : r _ { \lambda } \leq \rho _ { \lambda } \}$ at budget $\delta _ { w }$ , the power-side bridge of Theorem 3 being the step that needs the fuller good event $G _ { w } ;$ since $G _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } }$ supplies exactly $E _ { w }$ at the same budget, the validity statement holds verbatim with $\rho _ { \lambda }$ replaced by $\rho _ { \lambda } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } }$ , and the violation counts we report for the $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ arm, and for the SCoRE arm that shares the radius, are counts for the released radius. Because $G _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } }$ is cellwise, this domination is simultaneous over every union of cells at once, hence over all of Λ with no per-region union; that is why $L _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } }$ is indexed by K rather than by |Λ|. Neither radius dominates the other in general: in the well-resolved unsaturated regime the cellwise root terms of $\rho _ { \lambda } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } }$ carry an extra $\sqrt { L _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } } }$ relative to (6), while at fixed B and coarse, saturated, well-sampled configurations (for instance $K = 1 , \hat { p } = \hat { q } = 1 , n _ { w } = m _ { w }$ large, where $\rho _ { \lambda } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } } = O ( L _ { w } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } } / n _ { w } )$ against $\Theta ( n _ { w } ^ { - 1 / 2 } ) )$ it is the smaller of the two. Accordingly the power and sample-complexity statements (Theorem 3, Corollary $^ { 2 , }$ and the rate remark above) are proved for (6): a reader reproducing the rate analysis should use it, and a reader running the released code will obtain (8).

## F.3 Proof of Theorem 3 (power)

Lemma 5 (Power-side overshoot). Conditional on $D _ { w } , f o r$ each λ, with probability $\begin{array} { r } { \ge 1 - 2 \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } \colon \mathrm { U C B } ( \lambda ) \le \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ Z ^ { \lambda } ] + } \end{array}$ $\operatorname { R a d } ( \lambda )$ with $\mathrm { R a d } ( \lambda ) = 3 \sqrt { 2 \bar { V } _ { \lambda } \log ( 2 / \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } ) / n _ { r } } + 1 1 B \log ( 2 / \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } ) / ( n _ { r } - 1 )$ , where $\bar { V } _ { \lambda } = \mathrm { V a r } _ { P } ( Z ^ { \lambda } )$

Proof. Two events: (i) the lower Maurer–Pontil tail on the mean, applied to $- Z \colon \hat { G } ( \lambda ) \ : \ : \leq \ : \ : \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ Z ^ { \lambda } ] \ : \ : + \ :$ $\sqrt { 2 \hat { V } \log ( 2 / \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } ) / n _ { r } + 7 B \log ( 2 / \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } ) / ( 3 ( n _ { r } - 1 ) ) }$ with probability $\geq 1 - \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } ;$ (ii) empirical-to-population variance concentration for bounded variables: $\hat { V } ( \lambda ) \leq 2 \bar { V } _ { \lambda } + 4 B ^ { 2 } \log ( 2 / \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } ) / ( n _ { r } - 1 )$ with probability $\ge 1 - \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime }$ (the Maurer– Pontil sample-variance bound carries $n _ { r } - 1 )$ . Substituting (ii) into the UCB’s own radius term and collecting constants gives the display; the conversion of the two empirical-variance root terms to $\bar { V } _ { \lambda }$ costs the enlarged linear constant 11. □

Lemma 6 (Floor-side LCB deficit). Let $Y \in [ 0 , R ]$ be bounded with population variance $V = \operatorname { V a r } ( Y )$ , and let LCB be the Maurer–Pontil empirical-Bernstein lower confidence boundfor $\bar { \mathbb { E } } [ Y ]$ at level $\delta ^ { \prime }$ from $N \geq 2 i . i . d$ . draws. Then with $p r o b a b i l i t y \ge 1 - 2 \bar { \delta } ^ { \prime }$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } [ Y ] \mathrm { - L C B } \le 3 \sqrt { 2 V \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / N } + 1 1 R \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / ( N - 1 ) . } \end{array}
$$

Proof. This is Lemma 5 applied to −Y (lower endpoint instead of upper). Two events: (i) the Maurer–Pontil lower tail on the mean gives $\bar { Y } \ge \mathbb { E } [ Y ] - [ \sqrt { 2 \hat { V } \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / N + 7 R \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / ( 3 ( N - 1 ) ) } ]$ with probability $\ge 1 - \delta ^ { \prime } ;$ (ii) the empirical-to-population variance concentration $\hat { V } \le 2 V + 4 R ^ { 2 } \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / ( N - 1 )$ with probability $\geq 1 - \delta ^ { \prime }$ . Because the confidence endpoint subtracts its own radius, $\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } [ Y ] - \mathrm { L C B } = ( \mathbb { E } [ Y ] - \bar { Y } ) + ( \bar { Y } - \mathrm { L C B } ) \le 2 \sqrt { 2 \hat { V } \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / N + } } \end{array}$ $\scriptstyle { \frac { 1 4 } { 3 } } R \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / ( N - 1 )$ : the deficit is twice the radius (both terms doubled), not one radius. Substituting (ii) gives $2 \sqrt { 2 \hat { V } \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / N } \le 3 \sqrt { 2 V \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / N } + 4 \sqrt { 2 } R \log ( 2 / \delta ^ { \prime } ) / ( N - 1 )$ , and $4 { \sqrt { 2 } } + { \frac { 1 4 } { 3 } } < 1 1$ collects the display. (For a Bernoulli $Y = S _ { \lambda }$ a Clopper–Pearson lower bound is also valid, with $V = C _ { Q } ( 1 - C _ { Q } )$ exact and no separate variance event; its deficit constants differ from the displayed empirical-Bernstein ones, which are the route used by Algorithm 1 and Theorem 7.) □

Theorem (U2, restated). (ii) Deterministic, unconditional. At the population-mass split sizes $m _ { w } \stackrel { > } { \sim } K _ { \lambda _ { s } } \stackrel { - } { q } ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } + L _ { w } / ( \kappa s \bar { ) } ^ { 2 }$ and $n _ { w } \gtrsim B ^ { 2 } K _ { \lambda _ { s } } p ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } + B ^ { 2 } L _ { w } / ( \kappa s ) ^ { \hat { 2 } } + \log ( 4 K / \delta _ { w } ) / \hat { p _ { \mathrm { m i n } } }$ (the last term the Assumption 1(ii) all-cellfeasibilityfloor, neededfor the Proposition 1 Chernoffevent $\hat { p } _ { k } \ge p _ { k } / 2 )$ , with $n _ { r } , m _ { f }$ as in (i) and the deterministic envelope $\begin{array} { r } { \bar { V } _ { \lambda _ { s } } \leq \frac { 5 } { 4 } B C _ { Q } ( \bar { \lambda } _ { s } ) \lesssim B \beta } \end{array}$ , the Proposition 1 good event $G _ { w }$ secures $\rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } \leq \kappa s / 3 2$ at no extra confidence budget, so the certificate succeeds with probability $\geq 1 - \delta$ unconditionally (the bridge below).

Proof. By $\mathrm { L R } _ { \mathrm { m a r g i n } } ( s )$ pick $\lambda _ { s }$ with $C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) \in [ \beta + s / 4 , \beta ^ { * } - s / 4 ]$ and $- G _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) \geq \kappa s / 8$ . On $E _ { w }$ (Proposition 1): $\mathbb { E } _ { P } [ Z ^ { \lambda _ { s } } ] \le G _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) + \rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } \le - \kappa s / 8 + \rho _ { \lambda _ { s } }$ . Risk-pass requires $\mathrm { U C B } ( \lambda _ { s } ) \le - \rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } ;$ by Lemma 5 it suffices that $- \kappa s / 8 + \rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } + \mathrm { R a d } ( \lambda _ { s } ) \stackrel { - } { \le } - \rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } , \mathrm { i . e . } \mathrm { R a d } ( \lambda _ { s } ) \le \kappa s / 8 - 2 \rho _ { \lambda _ { s } }$ , and with the nuisance condition $\rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } \leq \kappa s / 3 2$ this reduces to Ra $1 ( \lambda _ { s } ) \le \kappa s / 1 6$ , which the risk condition guarantees (variance and range terms each $\le \kappa s / 3 2$ for C large enough). The two Lemma-5 tails are folded by setting $\delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } = \delta / ( 8 | \Lambda | ) \le \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime }$ , so the two power-side risk tails sum to exactly $\delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } = \delta / ( 4 | \Lambda | )$ (this per-tail allocation is internal to the power proof and need not equal the validity budget split $\bar { \delta _ { w } } \doteq \delta / 2 , \bar { \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } } = \bar { \delta } _ { f } ^ { \prime } = \bar { \delta ^ { \prime } } ( 4 | \Lambda | )$ of Theorem 2’s budget split that both certificates share). The half-levels $\delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime }$ and (below) $\delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } / 2$ are virtual endpoints internal to this power proof: the released algorithm calibrates its risk UCB at the full $\check { \delta } _ { r } ^ { \prime }$ and its floor LCB at the full $\delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ , as Algorithm 1 displays. Remark 15 supplies the monotone step that carries the conclusion from the virtual endpoints to the released ones, so the sample-size conditions proved here are sufficient for the algorithm as implemented. Floor-pass: $C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) - \beta \geq s / 4$ , and by Lemma 6 applied at per-tail level $\delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } / 2$ (to $Y = S _ { \lambda _ { s } } \in [ 0 , 1 ] ,$ , so $R = 1$ and $V = C _ { Q } ( 1 \dot { - } C _ { Q } ) \leq C _ { Q } )$ the floor-LCB deficit is at most $3 \sqrt { 2 C _ { Q } ( 1 - C _ { Q } ) \log ( 4 / \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } ) / m _ { f } } + 1 1 \log ( 4 / \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } ) / ( m _ { f } - 1 ) \leq s / 4$ under the floor condition: each term is $\leq s / 8$ for C large, the linear Bernstein term carried explicitly and dominated by the $\beta / s ^ { 2 }$ term precisely because $s \leq c _ { 0 } \beta \leq \beta$ , and $\bar { C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) } \le \beta ^ { * } = \beta + s \le 2 \beta$ . The two Lemma-6 tails (mean deviation and variance concentration) sum to $\delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ via the half-split (whence the $\log ( 4 / \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } ) )$ ; the doubling of the deficit relative to a single radius is what the enlarged constants and absolute $C$ absorb, exactly as on the risk side (Lemma 5). Unconditionally (form (ii)), a union over the three events $E _ { w } , E _ { r } , E _ { f }$ certifies with probability $\geq 1 - \delta _ { w } - \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } - \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } \geq 1 - \delta ;$ conditionally on a realization $D _ { w } \in E _ { w }$ (form (i)), $E _ { w }$ is already secured, so only the two test tails $E _ { r } , E _ { f }$ enter and the conditional refusal probability $ { \mathbf { i s } } \leq \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } + \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ (the $\delta _ { w }$ term reappears only when marginalizing back to the unconditional statement, as in the theorem’s form-(i) display). The histogram form of the nuisance condition is Proposition 1 solved for the split sizes, with the source term carrying $p ( A _ { \lambda } )$ , not $q ( A _ { \lambda } ) ;$ under additional two-sided overlap w $\geq 1 / B$ one may substitute $p ( A ) \leq B q ( A )$ , giving an all-q form at a $B ^ { 3 }$ prefactor on the source split. 口

Remark 15 (Virtual half-levels versus the released endpoints). The power proof above evaluates the risk endpoint at $\delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } = \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } / 2$ and the floor endpoint at $\delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } / 2$ , because each side folds two Lemma tails (a mean deviation and a variance concentration) into one budget. The released implementation calibrates both tests at the $f u l l$ levels $\delta _ { r } ^ { \prime }$ and $\delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ . The gap is closed by monotonicity of the endpoints in the confidence level, not by re-running anything. Both endpoints are of the Maurer–Pontil form whose radius is increasing in $\log ( 2 / \gamma )$ , hence decreasing in γ: for $\gamma _ { 1 } \leq \gamma _ { 2 }$

$$
\mathrm { U C B } _ { \gamma _ { 2 } } ( \lambda ) \le \mathrm { U C B } _ { \gamma _ { 1 } } ( \lambda ) , \qquad \mathrm { L C B } _ { \gamma _ { 2 } } ( \lambda ) \ge \mathrm { L C B } _ { \gamma _ { 1 } } ( \lambda ) ,
$$

on every realization. Applying this with $\gamma _ { 1 } = \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime \prime } , \gamma _ { 2 } = \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime }$ and with $\gamma _ { 1 } = \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } / 2 , \gamma _ { 2 } = \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ : whenever the witness $\lambda _ { s }$ passes the risk test at the virtual level $( \mathrm { U C B } _ { \delta _ { s } ^ { \prime \prime } } ( \lambda _ { s } ) \le - \rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } )$ it also passes at the released level, and whenever it passes the floor test at the virtual level $( \mathrm { L C B } _ { \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } / 2 } \dot { ( } \lambda _ { s } ) \geq \beta )$ it also passes at the released level. The certification event proved above is therefore contained in the certification event of the released algorithm, so the displayed sample-size conditions are sufficient for the algorithm as implemented, with the same probability bound. Validity is unaffected and needs no such step: Theorem 2 is proved at the full levels $\delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } , \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ , which are exactly the levels the implementation uses. The direction matters and only runs one way: a procedure calibrated at the full level certifies at least as often as one calibrated at the half level, so power transfers from the virtual endpoints to the released ones, while validity is established directly at the released ones.

From random budgets to deterministic, world-dependent split-size envelopes. Theorem 3 is stated at the realized budget $\rho _ { \lambda _ { s } }$ and the estimated-weight variance proxy $\bar { V } _ { \lambda , { s } }$ , both random; we record the bridge to the displayed determin istic split sizes on the single Proposition 1 good event $G _ { w }$ (the full event of the proposition’s proof, $\mathbb { P } ( \mathbf { \dot { \boldsymbol { G } } } _ { w } ) \geq 1 - \delta _ { w } )$ with no extra confidence budget. These envelopes are world-dependent (they involve the witness $\lambda _ { s }$ and its population masses), hence deterministic sufficient sizes rather than a computable stopping rule; the realized budget $\rho _ { \lambda _ { s } }$ itself is computable from data via Proposition 1. Part (iii) of Proposition 1 gives only the validity-side domination $q ( A ) \leq { \tilde { q } } ( A )$ $p ( A ) \leq { \tilde { p } } ( A )$ ; the power side needs the reverse direction, which is already available on $G _ { w }$ from the same Step-3 control (no new tail event): since $| \hat { q } ( A ) - q ( A ) | \le D _ { A } ^ { q } : = \sqrt { K _ { \lambda _ { s } } q ( A ) / m _ { w } } + \sqrt { 2 L _ { w } / m _ { w } }$ and likewise $| \hat { p } ( A ) - p ( A ) | \leq D _ { A } ^ { p }$ the plug-in budgets obey $\dot { \tilde { q } } ( \lambda ) = 2 \hat { q } ( \lambda ) + 4 L _ { w } \dot { / } m _ { w } \stackrel { . } { \leq } 2 \dot { q } ( \lambda ) + 2 D _ { A } ^ { q } + 4 \dot { L } _ { w } / \dot { m } _ { w }$ and $\tilde { p } ( A ) \le 2 p \bar { ( A ) } + 2 D _ { A } ^ { p } + 4 L _ { w } / n _ { w } ^ { . . . }$ The witness obeys the margin relation $\begin{array} { r } { \kappa s / 8 \le - G _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) = \mathbf { \dot { \mathbb { E } } } _ { Q } [ S _ { \lambda _ { s } } ( \alpha - \eta ) ] \le C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) = q ( \hat { A } _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) } \end{array}$ , hence $\kappa s \leq 8 q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } )$ and, by bounded ratio, $\kappa s \leq 8 B p ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } )$ . Substituting these and the displayed split sizes $m _ { w } \gtrsim$ $( K _ { \lambda _ { s } } q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) + L _ { w } ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } , n _ { w } \gtrsim B ^ { 2 } ( K _ { \lambda _ { s } } p ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) + L _ { w } ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ into (6), each of the four budget terms is $O ( \kappa s / { \sqrt { C } } ) ;$ the split sizes give $K _ { \lambda _ { s } } q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) / m _ { w } \leq ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } / C$ and $L _ { w } / m _ { w } \le ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } / C$ separately, so $D _ { A _ { \lambda _ { s } } } ^ { q } \leq ( 1 + \sqrt { 2 } ) \kappa s / \sqrt { C } ;$ with $\kappa s \leq 8 q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } )$ and $q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) \leq 1$ this gives $\tilde { q } ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) \leq 2 q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) + 2 D _ { A _ { \lambda _ { s } } } ^ { q } + 4 L _ { w } / m _ { w } \leq \left( 2 + O ( 1 / \sqrt { C } ) \right) q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } )$ hence $\sqrt { K _ { \lambda _ { s } } \tilde { q } ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) / m _ { w } } = O ( \kappa s / \sqrt { C } )$ ; the source terms are identical under $\kappa s \leq 8 B p ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) ;$ so $\rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } ~ \le ~ \kappa s / 3 2$ on $G _ { w }$ for a large enough absolute constant C: the precise content of “Proposition 1 solved for the split sizes,” with no event outside $G _ { w } .$ For the variance, the clipped estimator (Algorithm 1; $0 \le \hat { w } \le B )$ and the importance identity $C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) = \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w S _ { \lambda _ { s } } ]$ give, on $G _ { w } , \bar { V } _ { \lambda _ { s } } \ ' \le \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \hat { w } ^ { 2 } S _ { \lambda _ { s } } ] \le \mathsf { \bar { B } } \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \hat { w } S _ { \lambda _ { s } } ] \le B ( C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) + \rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } )$ ; since $\kappa s \leq 8 C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } )$ forces $\rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } ~ \le ~ \kappa s / 3 2 ~ \le ~ C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) / 4$ , this is a deterministic envelope $\begin{array} { r } { \bar { V } _ { \lambda _ { s } } \ \le \ \frac { 5 } { 4 } B C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) \ \lesssim \ B \beta } \end{array}$ (using $\begin{array} { r } { C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) \leq \beta + \frac { 3 } { 4 } s \leq ( 1 + \frac { 3 } { 4 } c _ { 0 } ) \beta } \end{array}$ at the exact slack), so the $n _ { r }$ condition is deterministic of order at most $B \beta \log ( | \Lambda | / \delta ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } ;$ all tails fold into the single δ-budget of Proposition 1 and Theorem 2.

Matching discussion (binding language). Le Cam then forces refusal under the two displayed conditions. Upper. On $\mathcal { W } _ { B ^ { \prime } }$ the hypotheses of Theorem 3 hold $( \mathrm { L R } _ { \mathrm { m a r g i n } } ( s )$ from the aligned Λ, regular margin $( \kappa , s _ { 0 } )$ , Assumption 1, and the stated $\rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } \leq \kappa s / 3 2 )$ ; it gives $n _ { r } \asymp B \beta / ( \kappa s ) ^ { \top } ( \beta / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ at fixed B, via the deterministic envelope $\bar { V } _ { \lambda _ { s } } \dot { \leq } B C _ { Q } ( \lambda _ { s } ) \lesssim B \beta$ above) and $\dot { m } _ { f } \asymp \bar { \beta ^ { \intercal } } s ^ { 2 }$ , together with the weight splits $n _ { w } \asymp B ^ { 2 } K _ { \lambda _ { s } } p ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ and $m _ { w } \asymp K _ { \lambda _ { s } } q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ (plus $L _ { w } )$ . The totals add the weight splits, with the additive $L _ { w }$ and feasibility terms carried in full (not “up to logs,” since the bare $L _ { w } / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ or all-cell $p _ { \mathrm { m i n } }$ term can dominate): $n = n _ { r } + n _ { w } ^ { \ast } \asymp n _ { r } \operatorname * { i f f } B ^ { 2 } ( K _ { \lambda _ { s } } p ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) + \hat { L _ { w } } ) / ( \kappa \overline { { s } } ) ^ { 2 } + \log ( 4 K / \delta _ { w } ) / p _ { \operatorname* { m i n } } ^ { - \nu } \lesssim B \beta / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ , and $m = m _ { f } + m _ { w } \asymp m _ { f }$ iff $( K _ { \lambda _ { s } } q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) + L _ { w } ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } \lesssim \beta / s ^ { 2 } \colon$ sample-size comparisons distinct from the accuracy condition $\rho _ { \lambda _ { s } } \leq \kappa s / 3 2$ Both bounds run on the same pre-registered $( \kappa , \Lambda )$ of $\mathrm { m e s h } < s / 4$ , an existence statement for this s (not partitionor s-uniform), reconciling Theorem 5’s after-s lattice with Theorem $3 \mathrm { { } ^ { \circ } s }$ pre-registered one. The correspondence on the totals $( n , m )$ in the non-dominated regime is a pointwise (instance-dependent) rate match, not a uniform minimax statement: the upper split sizes depend on the unknown world through $p ( \tilde { A _ { \lambda _ { s } } } ) , q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) , K _ { \lambda _ { s } }$ , and $p _ { \mathrm { m i n } }$ , so the quantifier is $\forall W \exists N ( W , \bar { s } )$ , not $\exists N ( s ) \forall W$ (a class with $p _ { \operatorname* { m i n } { \mathrm { ~ \downarrow ~ } } }$ 0 makes the feasibility term $\log ( 4 K / \delta _ { w } ) / p _ { \mathrm { m i n } }$ unbounded). The weight axis is matched only on its K-free core (Theorem 8). □

Remark 16 (Misspecification honesty). If w is not K-measurable, the guarantee of Theorem 2 degrades gracefully: all statements hold with w replaced by its K-projection $\bar { w } = \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w \mid \bar { \mathcal { K } } ]$ and an additive model-bias term $b \kappa ( \lambda ) =$ $\vert \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ ( w - \bar { w } ) S _ { \lambda } ( \eta - \alpha ) ]$ added to $\rho _ { \lambda }$ . The bias $b _ { K }$ is not estimable from covariates alone and must be treated as an assumption, exactly parallel to the covariate-shift assumption itself. Validity is therefore stated as conditional on the shift model, with the same epistemic status as covariate shift.

Remark 17 (The shift model is a dial, not a disguise). Known-w (Model-A) supplies the exact function; here the K numbers $w _ { k } = q _ { k } / p _ { k }$ must be learned from finite samples, and the resulting nuisance floor is the real price; it appears explicitly in Theorem 3 and inflates the certifiable-slack threshold by $\gtrsim \rho _ { \lambda } / \kappa$ (an additive nuisance term; we do not pin its two-sided constant). Setting $K = 1$ forces $w \equiv 1$ (degenerate, no shift); growing K approaches general covariate shift at the cost of a $\sqrt { K }$ rate factor, with the feasibility constraint $K \log ( 4 K / \delta _ { w } ) \lesssim n _ { w } / 8$ of Assumption 1(ii) advertised, not hidden.

## F.4 Separation from the worst-case-box baseline

Proposition 4 (Separation). Consider the baseline that certifies λ iffthe worst-case reweighted accepted ratio over the whole weight box passes:

sup E<sub>P</sub>[w<sup>′</sup>S<sub>λ</sub>η]/E<sub>P</sub>[w<sup>′</sup>S<sub>λ</sub>] : w<sup>′</sup> K-measurable, $w ^ { \prime } \in [ 1 / B , B ] , \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w ^ { \prime } ] = 1 \} \ \leq \ \alpha$

(plus afloor LCB). There is an instance on which the baseline certifies no lattice point at the population level (every point fails either the box-risk test or the true floor), so on the floor-LCB validity event it refuses, and it can certify only via afloor-LCB overshoot, with probability at most $| \Lambda | \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ ; meanwhile Algorithm 1 certifies atfinite, explicit samples by Theorems 2–3.

Proof. Take $B = 5 , \alpha = 0 . 2 ;$ accepted cells with $( p , \eta ) \ : = \ : ( 0 . 2 5 , 0 . 1 0 )$ and $( 0 . 2 5 , 0 . 2 8 ) $ ; rejected cells of total mass 0.5 carrying $\eta \ : = \ : 1$ , placed immediately above the accepted cells; true $w \equiv 1$ The true accepted ratio is $( 0 . 2 5 \cdot 0 . 1 0 + 0 . 2 5 \cdot 0 . 2 8 ) / 0 . 5 \ : = \ : 0 . 1 9 \ : < \ : \alpha$ , with slack. Fix floor $\beta ~ = ~ 0 . 4 9$ and a nested-threshold lattice whose accepting point takes exactly these two cells: coverage $D = 0 . 5 \geq \beta$ (floor cleared) and risk budget $\varphi _ { Q } =$ $\mathbb { E } _ { Q } \big [ S _ { \lambda } \big ( \alpha - \eta \big ) \big ] \stackrel { - } { = } 0 . 2 5 ( 0 . 2 - 0 . 1 ) + 0 . 2 5 ( 0 . 2 - 0 . 2 8 ) \stackrel { - } { = } 0 . 0 0 5 > 0 ,$ , so $R _ { Q } = 0 . 1 9 \leq \alpha$ holds with margin. The relaxed frontier sits at $\beta ^ { * } = 0 . 5 0 6 2 5$ ; we take the lattice to also contain the threshold accepting an additional 0.00625 of the adjacent loss-one mass (split its first cell), so the lattice frontier coincides with it, $\beta _ { \Lambda } ^ { * } = 0 . 5 0 6 2 5$ and the operative slack is $s = \beta _ { \Lambda } ^ { * } - \beta = 0 . 0 1 6 2 5$ (this extra high-risk threshold only worsens the box-baseline’s worst-case ratio, so it does not help the baseline). The increasing rearrangement is $\dot { \eta } _ { O } ^ { * } = 0 . 1 0$ on [0, 0.25), 0.28 on [0.25, 0.5), and 1 on [0.5, 1], so over the left-frontier neighborhood $[ \beta ^ { * } - s , \beta ^ { * } ] = \mathsf { \check { \Gamma } } [ 0 . 4 9 , 0 . 5 0 6 2 5 ]$ ] the binding sub-interval $[ 0 . 4 9 , 0 . 5 )$ has margin $\eta _ { O } ^ { * } - \alpha = 0 . 2 8 - 0 . 2 = 0 . 0 8$ (the segment $[ 0 . 5 , \beta ^ { * } ]$ has the larger margin 0.8); hence the regular margin is $\kappa = 0 . 0 \tilde { 8 }$ , not 0.8. The risk-margin hypothesis of Theorem 3 is still discharged at the witness of coverage $0 . 5 , \varphi _ { Q } = 0 . 0 0 5 \geq \kappa s / 8 = 0 . 0 0 0 1 6 2 5$ , as is the floor; only the constants in the required sample sizes grow. The baseline’s adversarial reweighting is feasible $( w ^ { \prime } = 0 . 2$ on the good cell, 3.4 on the bad cell, 0.2 on the rejected mass; all values in $[ 0 . 2 , 5 ]$ and $\mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \mathbf { \bar { \boldsymbol { w } } } ^ { \prime } ] = 0 . 2 5 \cdot 0 . 2 + 0 . 2 5 \cdot 3 . 4 + \mathbf { \bar { 0 } . 5 } \cdot 0 . 2 = 1 )$ and yields accepted ratio $0 . 2 \dot { 5 } ( 0 . 2 \cdot 0 . 1 0 + 3 . 4 \cdot 0 . 2 8 ) \big / ( 0 . 2 5 ( 0 . 2 + 3 \cdot 4 ) ) = 0 . 2 4 3 / 0 . 9 0 = 0 . 2 7 > \alpha$ The same reweighting defeats every coverage-≥ β lattice point: accepting any of the $\eta = 1$ rejected cells only raises the worst-case accepted ratio, while dropping either accepted cell leaves coverage below $\beta = 0 . 4 9$ . The only point with box-worst-case risk $\leq \alpha$ is the one accepting the single good cell $( \eta = 0$ .10 alone, box ratio 0.10 since a single homogeneous cell’s ratio is reweighting-invariant), but its true coverage is $0 . 2 5 < \beta$ , so it fails the floor at the population level. Hence no lattice point is both box-risk-feasible and floor-feasible in population. On the simultaneous floor-LCB validity event (probability $\ge 1 - | \Lambda | \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ , Theorem $2 \mathrm { { : } } \mathrm { { s } }$ floor step) the baseline therefore refuses every point; it can certify the good-cell-only point only by a floor-LCB overshoot of the true coverage 0.25 past $\beta ,$ an event of probability $\leq | \Lambda | \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ The budgeted certificate, by contrast, certifies the displayed coverage-0.5 point once $\rho _ { \lambda } \le \kappa s / 3 2$ and the risk and floor conditions of Theorem 3 hold. □

The baseline uses no data about w: it optimizes over its own uncertainty set instead of estimating localized weights, and it is a distinct object from the budgeted test (the budgeted test never passes as $\rho _ { \lambda }  \infty )$ . Its uncertainty set is the two-sided box $w ^ { \prime } \in [ 1 / B , B ]$ , a baseline modeling choice distinct from the paper’s one-sided class $w \in [ 0 , B ]$ (widening the box to $[ 0 , B ]$ only enlarges the worst case, so it only strengthens the separation). The comparison scope is deliberately qualified: we do not claim the baseline is never more powerful in finite samples. The defensible statement is that whenever the baseline certifies with strict margin, the estimated-weight test also certifies once its nuisance and sampling radii are below that margin, and there exist instances (above) where the baseline certifies no population-feasible point (refusing with probability $\ge 1 - | \Lambda | \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } )$ while the estimated-weight test certifies with probability $\geq 1 - \delta$ . The DRO-box arm certifying 0/1,024 cells in $\ S \bar { 5 } . 2$ is the empirical face of this proposition.

## F.5 The SCoRE-inspired betting arm: self-contained validity

The SCoRE-inspired betting arm of §5.2 (Appendix G) replaces $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime \prime } \mathbf { s }$ empirical-Bernstein risk UCB by a weighted betting e-value, keeping every other component of Algorithm 1 (the wˆ, $\rho _ { \lambda }$ , weight-free floor LCB, four-block split, budget, and output rule) verbatim. It is an equal-budget risk-test ablation rather than a source-faithful implementation of Bai and Jin [2026], and the statement below is proved for the arm exactly as implemented. We state its validity self-contained.

Proposition 5 (Betting risk e-value for the SCoRE-inspired arm). Fix the pre-registered K and,from $D _ { w }$ , the clipped histogram wˆ and budgets $\rho _ { \lambda }$ of Algorithm 1; let $\hat { E _ { w } } \dot { = } \lbrace \forall \lambda : \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \left| \hat { w } \ \hat { - } \ w \right| \tilde { S _ { \lambda } } ] \leq \rho _ { \lambda } \rbrace$ be the Proposition 1 bias event $( \mathbb { P } ( E _ { w } ^ { c } ) \leq \delta _ { w } )$ . The proposition uses $\rho _ { \lambda }$ only through $E _ { w } ,$ , so it holds verbatimfor the released per-cell radius $\rho _ { \lambda } ^ { \mathrm { v 2 } } o f$ Remark 14, which supplies that event at the same budget; the reported SCoRE arm is the released one. For each $\lambda \in \Lambda$ put $Z _ { i } = \hat { w } ( X _ { i } ) \hat { S } _ { \lambda } ( X _ { i } ) ( L _ { i } - \alpha )$ and $U _ { i } = - ( \mathsf { \bar { Z } } _ { i } + \rho _ { \lambda } )$ on $D _ { r } ,$ , and over a $D _ { w }$ -measurable bet grid $\begin{array} { r } { \{ \theta _ { g } \} _ { g = 1 } ^ { G } \subset [ 0 , \theta _ { \operatorname* { m a x } } ] , \theta _ { \operatorname* { m a x } } = \frac { 1 } { 2 } \big ( ( 1 - \alpha ) B + \operatorname* { m a x } _ { \lambda } \rho _ { \lambda } \big ) ^ { - 1 } } \end{array}$ , define the mixture e-value

$$
\bar { E } _ { \lambda } \ : = \ : \frac { 1 } { G } \sum _ { g = 1 } ^ { G } \prod _ { i \in D _ { r } } \left( 1 + \theta _ { g } U _ { i } \right) .
$$

The SCoRE arm certifies risk at λ iff $\bar { E } _ { \lambda } \ge 1 / \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } ( \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } = \delta / ( 4 | \Lambda | ) )$ , tests the floor exactly as Algorithm 1 (Bernstein LCB on $D _ { f }$ at $\delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } = \delta / ( 4 \mathsf { | } \Lambda \mathsf { | } ) )$ , and outputs thefloor-LCB-maximal λ passing both, else ⊥. Then on $E _ { w } ,$ for every world with $G _ { Q } ( \lambda ) > 0 , \mathbb { E } [ \bar { E } _ { \lambda } \mid D _ { w } ] \leq 1 ,$ ; hence the arm is $( \alpha , \beta , \delta )$ -valid over the stratified-shift class (Theorem 2): the identical budget $\delta = \bar { \delta } _ { w } \dot { + } | \Lambda | \bar { \delta } _ { r } ^ { \prime } + | \Lambda | \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime }$ , only the risk test differing.

Proof. Conditional on $D _ { w }$ the points of $D _ { r }$ are i.i.d., and $U _ { i }$ is a function of $( X _ { i } , L _ { i } )$ and the $D _ { w }$ -fixed $( \hat { w } , \rho _ { \lambda } )$ ; so the $U _ { i }$ are i.i.d. given $D _ { w }$ , and for a fixed bet $\theta _ { g } \ge 0$

$$
\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } \Big [ \prod _ { i } ( 1 + \theta _ { g } U _ { i } ) \Big | D _ { w } \Big ] = \prod _ { i } \big ( 1 + \theta _ { g } \mathbb { E } [ U _ { i } \mid D _ { w } ] \big ) \le \exp \big ( \theta _ { g } n _ { r } \mathbb { E } [ U \mid D _ { w } ] \big ) , } \end{array}
$$

using $1 + x \leq e ^ { x }$ . On $E _ { w } , \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \hat { w } S _ { \lambda } ( L - \alpha ) ] = G _ { Q } ( \lambda ) + \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ ( \hat { w } - w ) S _ { \lambda } ( \eta - \alpha ) ] \ge G _ { Q } ( \lambda ) - \rho _ { \lambda } > - \rho _ { \lambda }$ for a riskunsafe world $( G _ { Q } ( \lambda ) > 0$ , |bias| ≤ ρ<sub>λ</sub>), so $\mathbb { E } [ U \mid D _ { w } ] = - ( \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \hat { w } S _ { \lambda } ( L - \alpha ) ] + \rho _ { \lambda } ) < 0$ and each factor’s conditional expectation is $\leq 1 ;$ averaging preserves $\mathbb { E } [ \bar { E } _ { \lambda } | D _ { w } ] \leq 1$ . By Ville/Markov, $\bar { \mathbb { P } } ( \bar { E } _ { \lambda } \geq 1 / \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } \mid D _ { w } ) \leq \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime }$ at a risk-unsafe $\lambda ;$ a union over the $\leq | \Lambda |$ scanned λ caps risk-side false certification at $| \Lambda | \delta _ { r } ^ { \prime } \dot { = } \delta / 4$ (selection among simultaneously valid e-value tests is free, as in Theorem 2). With $\mathbb { P } ( E _ { w } ^ { c } ) \le \delta _ { w } = \delta / 2$ and the weight-free floor bound at $| \Lambda | \delta _ { f } ^ { \prime } = \delta / \overset { \cdot } { 4 }$ (Theorem $2 \mathrm { { : } } \mathrm { { s } }$ floor step, unchanged), the total false-certification probability is $\leq \delta .$ . The grid range keeps every factor positive: the most negative $U _ { i } = - ( ( 1 - \alpha ) B + \rho _ { \lambda } ) \ge - 1 / ( 2 \theta _ { \operatorname* { m a x } } )$ , so $1 \dot { + } \theta _ { g } U _ { i } \ge 1 - \bar { \theta } _ { g } / ( 2 \theta _ { \mathrm { m a x } } ^ { - } ) \ge \frac { 1 } { 2 } > 0 . \stackrel { . . } { \bigsqcup } \quad \bigsqcup$

This is the formal arm behind the head-to-head of §5.2/Appendix G: a faithful batch (counts-computable, order-free) weighted e-value, with a per-sample sequential betting e-process the higher-fidelity follow-up. Its certify-frequency weakly dominates $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime \prime } \mathbf { s }$ on the audited grid (Appendix G); validity here is by Proposition 5, not by the 0 observed violations.

## G Additional Experimental Records

This appendix carries the protocol details and the appendix-level figures and tables referenced from §5.1 and §5.3.

## G.1 Synthetic protocol

The full validity grid (§5.2) is the B1 grid: 4 shift intensities × 256 parameter cells = 1,024 cells, 1,000 replications per cell (five higher-variance edge cells re-run at 2,000–8,000). Six arms run on every cell: oracle-A (known weights, run with the released two-family oracle kernel of Remark 10, not the three-family procedure displayed under Theorem 7), $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ (Algorithm 1, run with the released nuisance radius of Remark 14), and four demonstration arms: floor-free certification with a post-hoc floor check, weighted conformal [Tibshirani et al., 2019], plug-in (certify whenever point estimates pass), and the DRO worst-case box of Proposition 4. Violation accounting per arm: a certifying replication violates if the true $( R _ { Q } , C _ { Q } )$ of the certified λ breaks either constraint; Holm’s correction [Holm, 1979] runs across certifying cells within each arm; per-cell 95% Clopper–Pearson upper confidence bounds [Clopper and Pearson, 1934] are evaluated against $\delta _ { \mathrm { t o l } } = 0 . 0 6 2 5$ over powered cells (those with $\geq 4 7$ certifying replications), and the pooled CP-UCB is evaluated against $\delta = 0 . 0 5$ . These three quantities are reported separately throughout (Table 4). Generators draw conditional-risk profiles and shifted targets from registered families; world parameters, lattice grids, and acceptance bands were fixed before the runs they judge.

## G.2 Contour transfer: qualitative rate-shape only

Figure A1 records the contour-transfer analysis behind §5.1 (Claim C3), from the amended shape metrics, which are the authoritative reproducible record. Constants fit on the first intensity and held fixed track the shape and ordering of the certifiable boundary at higher intensities but miss its location by growing multiplicative factors; the strict pre-registered ±3% bands failed at all intensities.

## G.3 Localized geometry: recorded confounds

The localized-geometry evidence is in the main text (Figure 5, §5.1, Claim C4); two recorded confounds bound the claim’s scope. First, family-2 pooling: 162 of 640 worlds censor at the required-n cap and the pooled rank correlation drops to .503 even though within-pair ratio agreement is .995; the within-pair structure is the meaningful signal, and we report it as qualified. Second, the oracle-arm pooled correlations are not supportive of the localized functional, a range-constant artifact: the oracle arm’s required-n range is compressed by its much smaller constants (Table 4, bottom), leaving little rank variation for the functional to explain; we report this rather than excluding the arm.

## G.4 Two-axis CI-separation: failure at registered density, recovery at higher density

Figure A2 records the model-comparison run behind the first half of §5.1 (Claim C2): at the registered 41-cell density the additive form attains the best held-out point estimate (surface-CV $R ^ { 2 } = 0 . 9 2 0 2 )$ but the registered CI-separation criterion fails (intervals overlap), while re-running the same criterion at increasing design density resolves it; the criterion is met outright, and unanimously, by 635 cells (full numeric record: Appendix G.9). The 41-cell failure was statistical power, not non-identifiability.

## G.7 Lattice diagnostic

The discrete-lattice phenomenon of Remark 4 and Appendix D is observed empirically: on coarse-lattice profiles, zero-margin cells return required- $n = \infty$ (no sample size certifies within the cell), and log–log exponent fits across cell sweeps land strictly between −2 and 0, as the registered prediction states. This is a lattice-design diagnostic: it indicates the lattice misses the frontier’s margin, not that the map fails.

## G.8 Real-workload records and recorded deviations

The real-workload audit (§5.3) ran on frozen, cached pipeline outputs with the SLA scenario $( \alpha , \beta , \delta ) = ( . 1 0 , . 6 0 , . 0 5 )$ fixed before any data was described. Two deviations are on record, both availability-triggered and adjudicated under the registered escalation rule before any evaluation labels were read: leg 1 substituted Llama-3-8B [Grattafiori et al., 2024] for the originally registered model, and leg 2 re-entered with DeepSeek-V4-Flash as a conditional capability-ladder step, explicitly not independent validation, since it runs on the same corpus with the same eval set. DeepSeek-V4-Flash had no citable public technical report at write time; it is therefore identified by name and by the registered deviation record rather than by a bibliography entry, and its inference outputs are hash-bound in the archived frozen cache (Appendix G.11). An access guard logged evaluation-label reads; the logs contain exactly one access, by the final evaluation. The post-hoc frontier diagnosis uses 500 bootstrap resamples over evaluation items; the plug-in baseline’s claim and violation rates are computed over its 1,000 per-leg certification replications (distinct from the 500 frontier bootstraps), leg-split.

## G.9 Extended empirical records: multi-family bite, $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ envelope, characterized failures

These records extend the main-text claims with the breadth, envelope, and failure-mode characterizations; every number traces to a committed result file.

Multi-family bite (Claim C1 breadth). The labeled-axis bite (required-n vs. slack, oracle arm, floor freed) was re-run across three structurally distinct families; the steep $s ^ { - 2 }$ form holds across all three, the exact −2 being specific to the regular-margin family. Onset $\mathrm { p r e m i u m } = \mathrm { o n s e t _ { B ^ { \prime } } } ( K ) / \mathrm { o n s e t _ { o r a c l e } } ,$ log–log; fitted slope 0.955, 95% CI [0.755, 1.154] ∋ 1, against a reference slope-1 line.
