The qualitative account (required-n $\propto \bar { V } / \mathrm { m a r g i n } ^ { 2 }$ , so a sub-linear margin yields a shallower exponent) reproduces the ordering but not the exact exponent, which additionally carries finite-sample range terms. Slope robustness (registered continuumfamily). The continuum fit rests on 9 slack design points; a deterministic pairs bootstrap (seed 20260624, 20,000 resamples) returns a 95% slope $\operatorname { C I } \left[ - 2 . 1 1 , - 1 . 8 9 \right]$ (median −2.005), consistent with the OLS normalapproximation $\operatorname { C I } \left[ - 2 . 1 \bar { 4 } , - 1 . 8 6 \right]$ ], while the log-space residuals $\mathrm { s t a y } \leq 0 . 1 0$ in magnitude and the quadratic-curvature CI contains 0: no systematic departure from the linear log–log law. The two smallest-but-one slacks (0.020, 0.017) resolve to the same rung of the required-n search ladder (both 149,383, while the smallest slack 0.014 resolves to a higher rung, so this is a grid coincidence and not a ceiling), a mild flattening that does not bend the fit out of band.

B<sup>′</sup> certifiable envelope and the K-premium (Claim C5; companion to Theorem 8). B<sup>′</sup> certifies across the partition sweep $K \in \{ 4 , 8 , 1 6 , 3 2 , 6 4 \}$ ; its onset premium over oracle-A grows as $K ^ { 0 . 9 5 5 }$ (95% CI [0.755, 1.154] ∋ 1, Figure A4), while the declared bound B is second-order: onset is B-insensitive when the true ratios sit inside the bound and grows only as $B ^ { 0 . 6 2 }$ when they approach it (bounded-ratio coupling: heavy weight forces small source mass $p ( A ) \propto 1 / B _ { ☉ }$ cancelling a power of $\dot { B ^ { \mathrm { ~ } } } \mathrm { i n } \dot { \mathrm { U } } 2 ^ { \prime } \mathrm { s } B ^ { 2 } K \dot { p } ( A ) )$ ). The B<sup>′</sup> labeled onset obeys the same $s ^ { - 2 }$ bite as oracle-A (slope −2.005, $\operatorname { C I } \left[ - 2 . 2 0 , - 1 . 8 1 \right] )$ . At $\kappa s = 0 . 0 4 2 , K = 4$ the envelope run sits in the same regime as the main-text constants (it does not re-derive them: the two use different onset conventions): its cert-frequenc $y - \geq \frac 1 2$ onsets (oracle-A 6,367, B<sup>′</sup> 718,459) fall inside the Table 4 first-to-full-certification ranges (4,096–16,384 and $5 2 4 , 2 8 \bar { 8 } \mathrm { - } \mathrm { \sim } 1 0 ^ { 6 } )$ , and the plotted premium is measured on this $\geq \frac { 1 } { 2 }$ onset $( \approx 1 1 3 \times$ at $K = 4$ , the same order as the 64× full-certification ratio).

CI-separation recovers at higher design density (Claim C2). On the registered 41-cell $s ^ { * } ( n , m )$ surface the additive form has the clearly best held-out CV-RMSE center (0.0154, ≈ 30% below the nearest alternative, floor-free at 0.0220) but the bootstrap CIs overlap. Re-running the same registered model-comparison procedure (byte-identical criterion; only design density and Monte-Carlo depth raised: a pre-registration-allowed synthetic design axis, no formula or constant change) at increasing cell count, the probability of strict non-overlap against every alternative rises 0.12, 0.21, 0.29, 0.54, 0.58, 0.54, 0.75 at 40/55/70/90/110/130/134 cells (the registered-resolution run, 24 seeds per size) and continues 0.92, 0.96, 1.00 at 180/240/635 cells on a denser surface $( 1 3 ^ { 2 } \times 5$ design, 800 reps, 635 certified cells), with the additive↔floor-free gap growing monotonically $+ . 0 0 4  + . 0 1 6$ over this trajectory. At 635 cells the criterion is met outright: additive held-out CV-RMSE 0.0122 [0.0109, 0.0134] against the nearest (n-only / max-form) 0.0270 [0.0239, 0.0302] (a 2.2× separation with the alternative’s lower CI above the additive upper CI), and the verdict is unanimous across all 24 re-splits (Figure A2). The 41-cell failure was statistical power, not non-identifiability: the additive two-axis surface is the genuinely best fit, recovered exactly as the registered sample-limited reading predicted (the per-axis exponent-containment sub-test is a separate check, not revisited here).

Contour-constant drift is systematic (Claim C3). The contour factor errors $( \times 1 . 0 7 / 1 . 2 4 / 1 . 9 1 / 3 . 4 0$ at L1–L4, L1 in-sample) are strictly monotone in shift intensity (rank correlation 1.0) and grow ×3.2 from L1 to L4 while the accepted-region variance proxy grows only $\times 1 . 3 ;$ the log-correlation stays ≥ .889 at every intensity. The non-transfer is a predictable, intensity-driven constant inflation with rate-shape preserved, not an unstructured breakdown; the constants drift, the ordering and shape transfer.

Misspecification-to-breakage, exhibited in-class (the $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ conditional-validity boundary). The real-workload sensitivity grid (§5.3) reached only refusal-status invariance; a dedicated synthetic sweep exhibits the breakage boundary that $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime \prime } \mathbf { \bar { s } }$ K-measurability assumption guards. We redistribute target mass within each accepted K-cell toward its higher-η bins at fixed per-cell totals: an adversarial member of $\mathcal { W } _ { B } \left( w \bar { \leq } B = 1 0 \right.$ throughout the reported grid) that violates only K-measurability, the within-accepted-region w–η rank correlation rising from −0.87 (on-class) to +0.47. Because each cell total is preserved, the certificate’s per-cell masses, weights $\hat { w } _ { k }$ , and nuisance budget $\rho _ { \lambda }$ are unchanged, so $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ certifies the same aggressive near-frontier operating point at every perturbation strength (certify-frequency 1.0, spread 0.0 across the sweep); yet truth-level violations jump from 0 to 100% once the receding true frontier crosses that locked point (onset $\epsilon = 0 . 8$ at $n = 4 , 1 9 4 , 3 0 4 ;$ realized accepted risk $0 . 1 8 4  0 . 2 0 8$ past $\alpha = 0 . 2 { \mathrm { ~ a s ~ } } \beta ^ { * }$ falls $0 . 7 7 3 \substack {  0 . 7 \bar { 1 } 5 } )$ . The bias is invisible to the certificate and is not small-sample noise: at fixed $\epsilon = 1 . 0$ the violation rate rises with n $( 0 , 0 , 1 , 1$ at $n = 2 ^ { 1 8 } . . . 2 ^ { 2 4 } )$ , since more data only makes $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ confident enough to certify the aggressive point the hidden bias then breaks. A same-worlds control pins this on misspecification rather than infeasibility: oracle-A (Model-A, true per-bin w) records 0 violations at every ϵ. This is an adversarial sensitivity/breakage characterization (not an off-partition robustness guarantee and not a real-workload stratified-shift validation), and it confirms the disclosed conditionality of Theorem $\bar { 2 } { : }$ validity holds for K-measurable $w ,$ , and off-class the bias is not estimable from covariates alone (Remark 16).

B<sup>′</sup> positive certification: targeted existence check (companion to Claim C5). The full validity grid (§5.2) has B<sup>′</sup> certify only $^ { 8 / 1 , 0 2 4 }$ cells, a harsh-constants slice. This is a targeted existence check (not a breadth claim) that this low rate is not a fundamental inability of the implementable certificate. On the on-class kcell world $( K = 4$ $\log = 2 . 0 , B = 5 , \alpha = 0 . 2 0 .$ , a 50-point lattice, $n = m$ over $2 ^ { 1 5 } \dots 2 ^ { 2 2 }$ , 2,000 replications per cell), $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ positively certifies non-trivial operating points at both probed slacks: at $s = 0 . 2 0 \ : ( \beta = 0 . 6 9 1 )$ it first reaches certify-frequency 1.0 at $n = m = 2 6 2 , 1 4 4$ , with realized min true coverage $\approx 0 . 7 2 3$ and max true risk $\approx 0 . 1 5 0 ;$ at $s = 0 . 1 2 ( \beta = 0 . 7 7 1 )$ at $n = m = 1 , 0 4 8 , 5 7 6$ (the same onset as the main-text full-certification constant at this operating point), with min true coverage ≈ 0.805 and max true risk $\approx 0 . 1 7 5$ . Across all $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ certifying replications the violation count is 0/16,297, pooled $\mathbf { C P \mathrm { { - U C B } \ 1 . 8 \times 1 0 ^ { - 4 } } }$ . This denominator is certifying replications, so the interval is conditional on a certificate having been issued; $\delta$ bounds the unconditional failure probability and does not control this conditional rate, so we report the interval without comparing it to $\delta .$ . This is targeted synthetic existence evidence only: it does not show broad (multi-family/slack/K), practically easy (the onsets are large), or real-workload positive certification, and it does not overturn the 8/1,024 rate; it reads it. Oracle-A is a reference arm here $( 1 / 3 2 , 0 \bar { 0 } 0$ certifying replications violated, a single near-boundary risk-side event at true risk ≈ $\alpha ,$ pooled CP-UCB $1 . 5 \times 1 0 ^ { - 4 } )$ .

SCoRE-inspired betting arm: the equal-construction risk-test head-to-head. A SCoRE-inspired floor-augmented betting arm, in the spirit of Bai and Jin [2026], is evaluated here under identical conditions. We add it as a seventh arm whose $\hat { w } .$ localized nuisance budget $\rho _ { \lambda }$ , weight-free floor LCB, four-block split, confidence budget, and output rule are verbatim-identical to B<sup>′</sup> (Algorithm 1); the only difference is the risk test: a weighted betting e-value $\textstyle \prod _ { i } ( 1 ^ { - } + \lambda _ { \mathrm { b e t } } U _ { i } )$ $U _ { i } = - ( Z _ { i } + \rho _ { \lambda } )$ , mixed over a per-replicate bet grid, replaces $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime } \mathbf { \bar { s } }$ empirical-Bernstein UCB. Conditional on $D _ { w }$ the $D _ { r }$ points are i.i.d., so for a fixed bet $\begin{array} { r } { \mathbb { E } \big [ \prod _ { i } ( 1 + \lambda _ { \mathrm { b e t } } ^ { \sim } U _ { i } ) \bigm | D _ { w } \big ] = \prod _ { i } ( 1 + \lambda _ { \mathrm { b e t } } \mathbb { E } [ U _ { i } ] ) \le 1 } \end{array}$ under the unsafe null on the same weight-good event $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ uses; the mixture is valid by averaging and the union over $| \Lambda |$ matches $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime } \mathbf { \bar { s } }$ risk budget $\delta / 4$ exactly (full statement and proof: Proposition 5; pre-registered). On the deep-audit subset (6 cells × 4,000 reps) SCoRE+floor records 0/24,000 violations (pooled CP-UCB $\bar { 1 } . 2 \times 1 0 ^ { - 4 } \leq \delta )$ ; across the 1,024-cell grid (matched reps) its certify-frequency $\mathrm { i s } \geq \mathbf { B } ^ { \prime } \mathbf { \bar { s } }$ in every cell, strictly greater in 8, with 0 violations and the same powered-cell count (a non-trivial-certification count of 10 vs $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime \prime } \mathbf { \bar { s } }$ 9 at equal reps; the headline $8 / 5 8 3$ figure uses the registered escalated reps, so this matched-reps run is the head-to-head, not a re-derivation of the headline). The reading: this equal-construction e-value arm is valid and, in this fixed matched-replication run, weakly dominates $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ in observed certify-frequency $( \geq$ in every cell, strictly > in 8) without enlarging the powered-cell set (a small, uniform margin); no paired test or interval is computed for that ordering, so it is an ordering observed in this Monte Carlo run and not an estimated power gap. $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime \prime } \mathbf { s }$ practical conservatism is shared by this alternative risk test; so the certificate’s contribution is the lower-bound theory, two-resource map, and explicitly priced nuisance, not a finite-sample power advantage. (Determinism check: with the wˆ/ρ/floor block identical, $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ and oracle-A reproduce their 0-violation tallies on these cells.)

Scope ofthe comparison. This arm is an equal-budget risk-test ablation inside $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime } \mathbf { \bar { s } }$ architecture, not a source-faithfu implementation of Bai and Jin [2026]. That work builds conformal risk-adjusted e-values for individual test instances and thresholds them (with a reweighted extension under covariate shift), whereas this arm keeps $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime \prime } \mathbf { s }$ weight estimator, nuisance radius, floor LCB, split, and output rule and bets on the calibration block; Proposition 5 proves validity for the arm as implemented, and no claim is made that it reproduces SCoRE’s selection rule or tuning. It is accordingly reported as the equal-construction alternative we tested against $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime } \mathbf { \bar { s } }$ risk test, which is what the lower-bound thesis needs, and not as the strongest available same-target baseline; a source-faithful SCoRE comparison with its own selection rule and configuration is not run here.

## G.10 Pre-registered future work

Of the three pre-registered directions, two are addressed in this work: multi-family bite sweeps on non-theorem-aligned families, and denser surface sweeps that resolve the C2 CI-separation criterion (now met at 635 cells) and characterize the systematic C3 contour drift (Appendix G.9); and one stands: an uncensored family-2 rerun with a fixed-rangeconstant oracle test. The open theory edges connect to active lines: the nuisance-necessity question is now partly resolved (Theorem 8, Appendix H: a K-free necessary core, lattice-valued, with unknown-η the remaining edge) and the misspecification breakage boundary is now exhibited empirically (the in-class breakage sweep, Appendix G.9); richer shift models beyond stratification [Laghuvarapu et al., 2026, Wang et al., 2025a, Xu et al., 2025a, Yang and Jin, 2026, Correia and Louizos, 2025], multigroup transfer [Ye and Li, 2026], and floor-aware extensions of deployment-facing risk control [Zollo et al., 2024, Wang et al., 2026].

## G.11 Reproducibility and compute

Every headline number in the paper was produced by a pipeline in which derived-report scripts regenerate all aggregates from raw run files and figure scripts read only those files, hard-coding no numbers; rather than shipping result files, the released artifact regenerates the synthetic ones from pinned seeds and the real frontier maps from the shipped frozen evaluation arrays (the real refusal tallies additionally require rebuilding the inference cache from the external model weights). The release carries a claim-to-command map; four of its entries need the specific subcommand that writes the artifact, rather than the parent command, and we state them here so the mapping is unambiguous: run\_m2\_b2 analyze writes the rank-correlation record (the sweeps must be run first, which the command hard-gates), run\_t1a bsweep\_tight is the sole producer of the tight-overlap B-sweep and is deliberately not part of run\_t1a all, run\_t3 breakage writes the misspecification-breakage record, and derived\_reports sensitivity writes the K/B/jitter sensitivity grid (the real-data lattice-stability record is the separate real.certify\_real stability). The post-hoc bootstrap interval on the bite slope is computed by a script shipped alongside the run outputs rather than by a run command. The synthetic suite runs at CPU scale (seconds to minutes per block); the two real legs consumed at most 2–4 GPU-days of cached frozen-pipeline inference in total, and the caches are hash-bound and archived read-only.

Generator and seeding (synthetic suite). All synthetic randomness derives from a single master seed $\sigma _ { 0 } = 2 0 2 6 0 6 1 1$ through NumPy SeedSequence spawning: each grid cell–replication pair draws an independent PCG64 stream keyed by (cell index, replication index), and each world-family draw is keyed by (family id, draw index), so every cell and replication is independently and reproducibly seeded. A world fixes a finite bin partition with a declared K-cell stratified structure (the $\mathbf { B } ^ { \prime }$ class K) at a per-intensity bounded-ratio class constant $B \in \{ 2 , 3 , 4 , 5 \}$ (one value per shift level L1–L4); on-class families set the density ratio cell-constant, off-class families perturb the ratio within cells (used only for the misspecification-direction sweeps). The B1 validity grid is 4 shift intensities × 256 cells × 1,000 replications, with per-cell levels $( \alpha , \delta ) = ( 0 . 2 , 0 . 0 5 )$ , a 50-point nested-threshold lattice, and the floor $\beta ,$ , frontier $\beta ^ { * }$ , and margin κ recorded per cell in the grid files; the deep-audit subset re-runs 6 cells at 4,000 replications. The certificate, arm, and generator definitions, the four pre-registration locks, and the per-cell grid files together fix every parameter behind the headline tables; the code, with the synthetic master seed pinned in it, and the text-free frozen real-data evaluation arrays with their numeric lock records are publicly available at https://github.com/yahiko-l/certify-or-refuse (a code-and-data release whose commands regenerate the synthetic results and the real frontier maps; reported result file are not shipped), while all datasets used are public (SQuAD and NewsQA via the MRQA distribution) and the synthetic worlds are fully parameterized here.

## H Is the Model-B<sup>′</sup> Nuisance Cost Minimax Necessary?

The open edge, and the answer. Theorem 3 certifies in Model-B<sup>′</sup> once the histogram split sizes satisfy the per-axis conditions

$$
m _ { w } \ \gtrsim \ \frac { K _ { \lambda _ { s } } q ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) } { ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } } , \ n _ { w } \ \gtrsim \ \frac { B ^ { 2 } K _ { \lambda _ { s } } p ( A _ { \lambda _ { s } } ) } { ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } } \qquad ( \mathrm { p l u s \ } L _ { w } \mathrm { - t e r m s } ) ,\tag{9}
$$

and Appendix F leaves the matching lower bound open. The honest resolution is PARTIAL: the K-bearing split sizes are not proved necessary and are partly architecture artifacts, a K-free necessary core is established (for lattice-valued certification, the standing output model of §3), and one two-sided edge stays open:

The K-bearing split sizes (9) are not demonstrated minimax necessary. (i) There is no separate population p-functional to estimate: by the identity below, the populationfeasibility and coverage functionals are independent ofthe source marginal p. Any lower bound matching the histogram’s $n _ { w }$ rate would therefore have to arise through the unknown-η labeled-source/alignment problem, not from identifying a standalone p-functional; the task does not chargefor p-estimation as a separate population axis (a procedure may still estimate p, and p does influence the finite-sample labeled cost through how often the label channel visits each cell). (ii) The factor K on both axes is the $\ell ^ { 1 }$ price of recovering the entire K-dimensional weight vector (Proposition 1), not an evident certification price: the established necessary core is K-free (the complete unknown-η rate remaining open): a target term $\Omega ( q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 } )$ (the floor m-axis localized; κ cancels) and a labeled-source term $\Omega \bar { ( V / ( \kappa s ) } ^ { 2 } )$ with $\bar { V } \le \mathbb { E } _ { P } ^ { } [ w ^ { 2 } S _ { \lambda } ]$ (the existing n-axis; worst-slice instances expected to attain $\bar { V } \asymp \bar { B ^ { 2 } p ( A ) } )$ (iii) The $K \mathrm { - } f r e e$ target bound is proved in the known-η submodel (Theorem $s ) ;$ the $K { \cdot } f r e e$ labeledsource bound is the existing (varying-η) n-axis ofTheorem 5, read as a reduction. Whether the full unknown-η Model-B<sup>′</sup> also avoids K (via a direct scalar-functional certificate) or instead reintroduces K through the unknown-η source↔target alignment (Theorem 6) is the one genuinely open edge.

We prove the K-free target lower bound (Theorem 8, rigorous), reduce the source lower bound to the map’s n-axis (§H.3), show in which sense the K in (9) is an architecture artifact (§H.4), and record the precise verdict and the open edge in §H.5.

## H.1 The structural identity that reframes the question

The entire analysis turns on one exact identity. In Model- $\cdot \mathbf { B ^ { \prime } } .$ , w is constant on each cell, so for any cell $\mathcal { X } _ { k }$ the within-cell target map equals the within-cell source map, $Q ( \cdot \mid \mathcal { X } _ { k } ) = P ( \cdot \mid \mathcal { X } _ { k } )$ ; writing $\eta _ { k } = \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \eta \mid \mathcal { X } _ { k } ]$ for the within-cell loss mean and $q _ { k } = Q _ { X } ( \mathcal { X } _ { k } )$ , the feasibility-deciding functional collapses to a target-mass-weighted sum:

$$
G _ { Q } ( \lambda ) = \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w S _ { \lambda } ( \eta - \alpha ) ] = \mathbb { E } _ { Q } [ S _ { \lambda } ( \eta - \alpha ) ] = \sum _ { k : \lambda _ { k } \subseteq A _ { \lambda } } q _ { k } ( \eta _ { k } - \alpha ) , \qquad C _ { Q } ( \lambda ) = \sum _ { k \subseteq A _ { \lambda } } q _ { k } .\tag{10}
$$

Both the feasibility verdict $( G _ { Q } \leq 0 )$ ) and the coverage depend only on the cell pairs $\left( q _ { k } , \eta _ { k } \right)$ , never on the source marginal p (here $\eta _ { k } = \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \eta \mid \dot { \mathcal { X } } _ { k } ]$ ; in the construction below $\eta$ is cellwise constant, so η is just its cell value). The histogram certificate of Algorithm 1 nonetheless estimates $G _ { Q }$ as a source expectation $\mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \dot { w } S _ { \lambda } ( L - \alpha ) ]$ , because labels L live only on the source side $( Q _ { X }$ is unlabeled), and therefore needs $\hat { w } _ { k } = \hat { q } _ { k } / \hat { p } _ { k }$ , hence both p (from $n _ { w }$ source covariates) and $q$ (from $m _ { w }$ target covariates). The p-dependence is thus a property of the reweighting estimator, not of the population certification functional (though p still enters the finite-sample labeled cost through how often the label channel visits each cell). This is the lever for the whole verdict.

Throughout we use the standing local regime $s \leq s _ { 0 } \land c _ { 0 } \beta _ { : }$ , regular margin $\kappa , \delta \leq 1 / 8 , B \geq 2$ , and the registeredpartition setting of Assumption $1 ;$ A denotes a critical accepted region (union of registered cells) with target mass $q ( A ) = \Theta ( \beta )$ pinned by the floor and source mass $p ( A )$

Consequently no $( \alpha , \beta , \delta )$ -valid lattice-valued procedure with $\delta \leq 1 / 8$ (per the standing definition of $\ S 3 { : }$ outputs a point of Λ or refuses; $W ^ { + }$ is lattice-infeasible by $( i i ) )$ certifies the feasible world with probability $\geq 1 / 2$ once

total target covariate sample $M = \Omega \Bigl ( \frac { q ( A ) } { s ^ { 2 } } \Bigr )$ is necessary.

The source covariate sample and the (Bernoulli) labels have identical laws in the two worlds (same $p ,$ same η, hence same $L \mid X \sim \operatorname { B e r n } ( \eta ) \bar { ) }$ , contributing zero KL. The target covariate sample is i.i.d. from $Q _ { X }$ , which differs only by moving mass ε between $\Phi _ { + }$ and Φ inside a region of $Q \cdot$ -mass $2 q _ { 0 } \asymp \beta$ . For a single target draw, with $Q ^ { \pm }$ the two laws,

$$
\chi ^ { 2 } ( Q ^ { + } \parallel Q ^ { - } ) = \sum _ { \mathrm { c e l l s } } \frac { ( q _ { k } ^ { + } - q _ { k } ^ { - } ) ^ { 2 } } { q _ { k } ^ { - } } = \frac { ( 2 \varepsilon ) ^ { 2 } } { q _ { 0 } - \varepsilon } + \frac { ( 2 \varepsilon ) ^ { 2 } } { q _ { 0 } + \varepsilon } \ \leq \ \frac { 8 \varepsilon ^ { 2 } } { q _ { 0 } - \varepsilon } \ \leq \ \frac { C \varepsilon ^ { 2 } } { q ( A ) } ,
$$

using $q _ { 0 } \geq { \frac { 1 } { 2 } } ( q ( A ) - g ) \asymp q ( A )$ and $\varepsilon \le q _ { 0 } / 2$ , with C absolute. Hence for M i.i.d. target draws (the procedure’s entire target sample, $m _ { w } + m _ { f } )$ $\mathrm { K L } ( W ^ { + } \| W ^ { - } ) \leq M \chi ^ { 2 } ( Q ^ { + } \| Q ^ { - } ) \leq C M \varepsilon ^ { 2 } / q ( A )$ . With the construction’s own choice $\varepsilon = s / 4$ this $\begin{array} { r } { \mathrm { i s } \le \frac { C } { 1 6 } M s ^ { 2 } / q ( A ) } \end{array}$ , so $M \leq c q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 }$ (with $c = 2 / C )$ gives $\mathrm { K L } \leq { \frac { 1 } { 8 } }$ and $\mathrm { T V } \leq { \frac { 1 } { 4 } }$ by Pinsker. This carries no K: the perturbation is a single scalar mass-transfer, so its detectability depends on $\varepsilon ^ { 2 } / q ( A )$ ), not on howfinely the band is partitioned.

The rate is $q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 }$ , not $q ( A ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } { : }$ the κ cancels. The two worlds must be separated to flip feasibility; the flip is driven by the functional gap $| G _ { Q } ^ { W ^ { + } } - G _ { Q } ^ { W ^ { - } } | = 4 \kappa \varepsilon$ , but the detectability is set by the mass-transfer ε alone (the $\chi ^ { 2 }$ depends on $\varepsilon ^ { 2 } / q ( A )$ , with no $\kappa )$ . To realize a slack-s feasible world one needs $\varepsilon = \Theta ( s )$ (so the coverage frontier sits $\Theta ( s )$ above $\beta )$ , independent of κ; hence the detection threshold is $\Theta ( q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 } )$ and the risk margin $\kappa \varepsilon = \Theta ( \kappa s )$ rides along automatically. This is precisely the floor m-axis of Theorem $5 ( m \gtrsim \beta / s ^ { 2 }$ , the $\sqrt { \beta }$ being intrinsic to the perturbable block of mass $\asymp \beta )$ , localized to $q ( A ) \asymp \beta$ , no κ, no K.

Le Cam step. If a valid A certified $W ^ { - } \ \mathrm { w . p . } \geq 1 / 2$ , then on the $\mathrm { T V } \leq 1 / 4$ -close $W ^ { + }$ it would certify (some lattice point) $\mathrm { w . p . } \geq 1 / 4 > \delta$ , contradicting validity in the lattice-infeasible $W ^ { + }$ (there every lattice point it could output is invalid: coverage $\ge \beta$ points violate $R _ { Q } \le \alpha$ , sub-floor points violate $C _ { Q } \geq \beta )$ . Hence $\mathcal { A }$ refuses the feasible $W ^ { \bar { - } } { \bf w . p }$ $\geq 1 / 2$ whenever $M \leq c q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 }$ □ □

Remark 18 (The target core is the floor m-axis localized, not a nuisance axis). Theorem 8 is the same Le Cam two-point mechanism as the m-axis of Theorem 5 (move target mass out of a safe block at $\chi ^ { 2 }$ cost $\asymp \varepsilon ^ { 2 } ,$ /(block mass)), now read on the localized accepted mass $q ( A ) \asymp \beta ,$ giving the identical rate $\Omega ( q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 } ) \dot { = } \Omega ( \beta / s ^ { 2 } )$ with neither κ nor $K$ . So the “target nuisance $\mathrm { a x i s } ^ { , , }$ of (9), stripped of its estimator-specific $K$ , is the floor m-axis the paper already proves; it is not a new necessary phenomenon. (The histogram’s $K q ( A ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ is a sufficient split size for full-vector recovery; this lower bound proves a K-free, κ-free necessary component $\dot { \Omega ( q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 } ) }$ for the certification task; it does not establish sufficiency, nor rule out a stronger lower bound elsewhere in the class.)

## H.3 The labeled-source axis is the existing n-axis, with no K

The certificate also needs source data, and the established necessary component is K-free. This subsection proves no new source-channel theorem; it records that the existing n-axis of Theorem 5 already supplies a K-free labeledsource lower bound, together with the standard shifted-slice envelope that surfaces its B-dependence. The aggregate risk estimand is $G _ { Q } = \overline { { \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w S _ { \lambda } ( L - \alpha ) ] } }$ ], a single bounded linear functional with population variance proxy $\tilde { V } =$ $\mathrm { V a r } _ { P } ( w S _ { \lambda } ( L - \alpha ) ) \le \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w ^ { 2 } S _ { \lambda } ] . \mathrm { ~ A ~ }$ natural estimator of its sign at margin κs from n i.i.d. source label-draws has estimator variance ${ \bar { V } } / n ;$ the matching lower bound, however, is not this variance heuristic but the n-axis Le Cam pair of Theorem 5 (Appendix B.1: raise the loss on an inframarginal accepted slice; per-sample $\mathrm { K L } \asymp \rho \Delta ^ { 2 }$ with $\Delta \asymp \kappa s / \rho$ over a slice of accepted weighted mass), which, inheriting that theorem’s hypotheses (regular margin, local regime $s \leq c _ { 0 } \beta , \delta \leq 1 / 8 )$ , is, in its shiftless $w \equiv 1$ form, already a formal lower bound: for that pair $\bar { V } _ { \mathrm { p a i r } } \breve { \asymp } \beta$ , giving

$$
n = \Omega \Bigl ( \frac { \bar { V } _ { \mathrm { p a i r } } } { ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } } \Bigr ) = \Omega \Bigl ( \frac { \beta } { ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } } \Bigr ) .
$$

We use this as a reduction, not a fresh proof, and do not claim a fully instance-dependent theorem for arbitrary $\bar { V }$ by renaming the variance. An analogous shifted worst-slice construction (placing the inframarginal slice on cells where $w \asymp B$ , admissible in $\mathcal { W } _ { B } )$ is expected to realize $\bar { V } _ { \mathrm { p a i r } } \asymp B ^ { 2 } p ( \tilde { A } )$ (consistent with the upper envelope $\mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w ^ { 2 } S _ { \lambda } ] \le B ^ { 2 } p ( A ) \wedge B q ( A )$ , the $B q ( A )$ form from $w ^ { 2 } S _ { \lambda } \stackrel {  } { \le } B w S _ { \lambda }$ with $\mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w S _ { \lambda } ] = q ( A ) )$ ), but that extension is stated, not re-derived here. This $\bar { V } / ( \kappa \bar { s } ) ^ { 2 }$ is the oracle (w-based) analogue of U2’s (estimated-weight) risk-test radius (Theorem $3 \colon n _ { r } \gtrsim \bar { V } _ { \lambda _ { s } } \log ( | \dot { \Lambda } | / \delta ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ with the estimated-weight proxy $\begin{array} { r } { \bar { V } _ { \lambda _ { s } } \leq \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \hat { w } ^ { 2 } S _ { \lambda _ { s } } ] \leq \bar { B } \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \hat { w } S _ { \lambda _ { s } } ] } \end{array}$ on the good event $G _ { w }$ the two agree up to the small nuisance bias $\le \kappa s / 3 2 , { \it t }$ Appendix F) and the same object as Claim 1’s n-axis; it carries no $K$ . So the source-covariate weight-split $n _ { w } \gtrsim B ^ { 2 } \dot { K } p ( A ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ of (9) is not charged by the certification task as a separate axis: by the identity (10) feasibility is p-independent, so a procedure need not estimate $p ;$ what it must pay on the labeled side is the K-free risk-test variance $\bar { V }$ , already on the $n / n _ { \ i }$ axis. (The one caveat, whether unknown η reintroduces a K-dependent labeled cost, is the open micro-question of §H.5.)

## H.4 U2’s linear K is a full-vector estimation price (architecture-specific, not proved necessary)

The K in both U2 split sizes enters through Proposition 1, which bounds thefull-vector $\ell ^ { 1 }$ weight error $\mathbb { E } _ { P } [ | \hat { w } - w | \mathbf { 1 } _ { A } ] \leq$ $2 \sqrt { K q ( A ) / m _ { w } } + 2 B \sqrt { K p ( A ) / n _ { w } }$ . The $\sqrt { K }$ is the $\ell ^ { 1 }$ price of estimating all $K _ { A }$ cell-ratios simultaneously (a $K \cdot$ parameter object), inherited by Algorithm 1 because its risk test plugs the whole vector wˆ into a reweighted source mean. But certification needs only the scalar $G _ { Q }$ . The two lower bounds above are $K \mathrm { - } f r e e .$ , which strongly suggests the K is not a certification cost, but the target bound (Theorem 8) is proved in a known-η submodel (its construction holds η fixed, putting all the signal in $q )$ , while the labeled-source bound is the existing varying-η n-axis read as a reduction (§H.3); neither resolves the joint unknown-η alignment, and a K-free target bound in the known-η submodel does not by itself rule out a K-dependent lower bound in the full unknown-η class. We therefore state the conclusion carefully:

• Target side. When η is known on the cells, the scalar $\textstyle \sum _ { k \in A } q _ { k } ( \eta _ { k } - \alpha )$ is estimable directly by the empirical mean of $( \eta - \alpha ) \mathbf { 1 } _ { A }$ over target covariates, no $K$ (the $\ell ^ { 1 }$ vector error over-counts by aggregating $K$ cellwise absolute deviations whose signed sum is all $G _ { Q }$ needs). On the Theorem $^ 8$ submodel $| \eta - \alpha | \le \kappa$ on the critical cells, so this estimator has variance $\leq \kappa ^ { 2 } q ( A ) / M$ and detects the feasibility $\displaystyle \mathrm { g a p } \asymp \kappa s$ once $M \gtrsim \kappa ^ { 2 } q ( A ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } = q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 }$ (for a fixed A at constant confidence; confidence and lattice-simultaneity logarithms suppressed), matching the $\Omega ( q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 } )$ lower bound (the κ cancels). For general known $\eta$ with $| \eta - \alpha | = \Theta ( 1 )$ the direct estimator still removes $K$ but retains $\kappa ^ { - 2 }$ , giving $q ( A ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ ; either way the histogram’s $\dot { K } \dot { q } ( A ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ carries the extra factor $K .$

• Source side. The aggregate $\mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w S _ { \lambda } ( L - \alpha ) ]$ has variance $\bar { V } \le \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w ^ { 2 } S _ { \lambda } ] \le B ^ { 2 } p ( A )$ (no $K ) ;$ the histogram’s $B ^ { 2 } K p ( A )$ is a factor $\asymp K$ above it. By the identity (10) the p-estimation behind the $\sqrt { K p ( A ) / n _ { w } }$ term is not needed as a separate population feasibility functional.

Hence (9) is a sufficient split-size pair whose K-factor is not proved necessary, and whose source-covariate split carries no separatepopulation necessity (population feasibility carries no p-functional, so the task does not charge a standalone p-estimation axis; any matching $n _ { w }$ necessity would route through the unknown-η alignment). What is not settled is whether a procedure that must be valid for unknown η can avoid the $K \colon$ : unknown η couples the source-label and target-covariate channels (the alignment mechanism of Theorem 6), and could in principle re-introduce a K-dependent labeled cost. So the precise status is $^ { * } K$ is architecture-specific and reducible when η is resolvable; whether it is reducible uniformly over unknown-η Model- ${ \bf - B ^ { \prime } }$ is open” (§H.5), not the unconditional “U2 overcharges by $K ^ { \ast }$

Remark 19 (What the K buys, and why U2 still pays it). The histogram’s K is not wasted: it yields a simultaneous guarantee over the whole lattice $\left\{ A _ { \lambda } \right\}$ , a computable wˆ for the axis-attribution diagnostics of $\ S 4 . 5$ , and it sidesteps needing $\eta$ known on cells (it learns the reweighting from covariates and lets the empirical-Bernstein risk test absorb the residual). The K-free scalar route requires $\eta$ resolved per cell (folded into the labeled axis at variance $\bar { V } )$ or a direct functional estimator. The two are not contradictory: U2 is a valid sufficient condition, and Theorem 8 shows its K-factor is not demonstrably necessary, with the honest caveat that the matching K-free upper bound for unknown η is not in hand.

## H.5 Verdict, exact reach, residual gap

Verdict: PARTIAL. The K-bearing histogram rate is not proved necessary and is provably architecture-specific in part; a K-free necessary core is established (for lattice-valued certification: §3’s standing output model, every procedure outputting a point of Λ or refusing; the present bad world $W ^ { + }$ is relaxed-feasible, so the lattice restriction is genuinely load-bearing here, unlike the relaxed-infeasible constructions of Theorems 5 and 6); whether full unknown-η Model-B<sup>′</sup> forces K back is the open edge. What is settled.

• No population-level lower bound can force the source-covariate weight-split (via the identity); this does not by itself exclude a finite-sample unknown-η lower bound (the open edge below). By (10) the population feasibility and coverage are functionals of $( q , \eta )$ alone; the source marginal p never enters. So the $n _ { w } \gtrsim B ^ { 2 } K p ( A ) \dot { / } ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ covariate split of (9) is not a population-level information-theoretic necessity for the certification task; it is an artifact of the reweighting estimator (the population task does not charge for p-estimation as a separate axis, though p still affects the finite-sample labeled cost through how often labels visit each cell, and a procedure may still estimate it). (The necessary labeled-source cost is the K-free risk-test variance V<sup>¯</sup> , §H.3.)

• A K-free, κ-free target lower bound holds (Theorem 8, rigorous, unconditional within the construction). $\Omega ( q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 } )$ total target covariate samples are necessary: the floor m-axis of Theorem 5 localized to $q ( A ) \asymp \beta ,$ not a new nuisance axis (Remark 18). The histogram’s $K q ( A ) / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 }$ is a $\mathrm { f a c t o r } \asymp K / \kappa ^ { 2 }$ above the proven necessity.

• A K-free labeled-source lower bound holds by reduction, at fixed B (§H.3). The existing n-axis gives $\Omega ( \bar { V } / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } )$ with $\bar { V } \le \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ w ^ { 2 } S _ { \lambda } ]$ ], K-free (U2’s risk-test radius and Claim 1’s n-axis). The explicit worstslice envelope $\bar { V } \asymp B ^ { 2 } p ( A )$ (and the form $\bar { V } \leq B q ( A ) )$ is stated as a worst-slice placement, not re-derived in full here.

What is not settled (the honest open edge). The K-free target lower bound is proved in the known-η submodel (the construction fixes η to isolate q); the K-free labeled-source bound is the existing varying-η n-axis read as a reduction (§H.3). Neither proves the histogram K is unnecessary for thefull Model-B<sup>′</sup>, where η is unknown and must itself be learned on the cells:

• Lower-bound side. Unknown η couples the source-label channel (which sees η) and the target-covariate channel (which sees $q ) ;$ their alignment is exactly the correlation-detection mechanism that makes Model-B inconsistent (Theorem 6, Lemma 2). It is conceivable (though we have neither a proof nor a refutation) that a fully unknown-η construction reintroduces a K-dependent lower bound. So we do not claim the K is unnecessary in full Model-B<sup>′</sup>; we claim it is unnecessary once η is resolvable, and that no lower bound can force the source-covariate (p-estimation) split, the latter unconditionally at the population level, since feasibility carries no p-functional. (We do not assert a finite-sample unknown-η certificate that avoids all p-estimation; constructing one is part of the open upper-bound edge.)

• Upper-bound side. Matching the K-free lower bounds requires a direct scalar-functional certificate (average $( \eta - \alpha ) \mathbf { 1 } _ { A }$ on the target side, or fold a per-cell η-plug-in into the labeled axis) that is valid uniformly over the K-measurable Model-B<sup>′</sup> subclass $\{ \dot { W } \in \mathcal { W } _ { B } : \overset { \cdot } { \boldsymbol { \ i } }$ K-measurable} (not all of $\mathcal { W } _ { B }$ , which is inconsistent by Theorem 6), lattice-simultaneous, and genuinely K-free in finite samples. We conjecture it exists but do not construct it here.

Thus the open item is two-sided and sharply located: does unknown-η Model-B<sup>′</sup> admit a K-free certificate (upper), and ifnot, is there a K-dependent lower bound (lower)? The contribution of Theorem 8 is to show the question is not settled by the histogram split sizes: their K is a sufficient-condition factor not proved necessary, the established necessary core is K-free and coincides with the map’s own two axes localized, and no lower bound charges a separate source-covariate (p-estimation) split (population feasibility carries no p-functional).

Net for the paper. The honest upgrade is to replace “nuisance necessity open” with: the Model-B<sup>′</sup> histogram split sizes are not proved minimax necessary, and are partly architecture artifacts: by the identity $\begin{array} { r } { G _ { Q } = \sum _ { k } \hat { q _ { k } } \bigl ( \hat { \eta _ { k } } - \hat { \alpha } \bigr ) } \end{array}$ populationfeasibility is p-independent, so there is no separate population source-covariate (p-estimation) necessity (any matching $n _ { w }$ necessity would have to route through the unknown-η alignment, not a standalone p-functional), and the factor K on both axes is the ℓ<sup>1</sup> full-weight-vector price of Proposition 1. The information-theoretically necessary core is K-free and carries no separate nuisance axis: a target term $\Omega ( q ( A ) / s ^ { 2 } )$ (Theorem 8; the floor m-axis localized, κ cancels) and a labeled-source term $\Omega ( \bar { V } / ( \kappa s ) ^ { 2 } ) , ~ \bar { V } \le \mathbb { E } _ { P } [ \dot { w } ^ { 2 } \dot { S } _ { \lambda } ]$ with worst-slice instances expected to attain $\bar { V } \asymp B ^ { 2 } p ( A )$ (the existing n-axis). The genuinely open edge, now sharply located, is whether the full unknown-η Model-B<sup>′</sup> admits a K-free certificate (a direct scalar-functional upper bound, in the spirit of covariate-shifted mean estimation [Adil and Błasiok, 2026]) or insteadforces a K-dependent lower bound through the unknown-η source↔target alignment (Theorem 6’s mechanism).

One-line summary. B<sup>′</sup> nuisance “necessity” is mostly negative: there is no separate population source-covariate necessity (population feasibility is p-independent, $\begin{array} { r } { G _ { Q } = \sum _ { k } q _ { k } ( \eta _ { k } - \alpha ) } \end{array}$ ; any matching $n _ { w }$ necessity would route through the unknown-η alignment), and the histogram K is an $\ell ^ { 1 } f u l l .$ -vector price matchedfrom below by a K-free, κ-free target bound $\Omega ( \stackrel { \cdot } { q } ( A ) / s ^ { 2 } )$ (the map’s localized m-axis) plus the K-free labeled n-axis; the one remaining open edge is whether unknown-η Model-B<sup>′</sup> admits a matching K-free certificate or reintroduces K via the unknown-η alignment, a two-sided question this draft localizes rather thanfully closes.
